This in itself is undemocratic, in that it makes a distinction between the value of family life for one set of people as over against another; or, rather, claims that one set of people are of so much less importance than another, that a valuable side of life pertaining to them should be sacrificed for the other.

===Chinese===
家务雇主出于热忱，要保全自己的家庭生活完整无扰，行为便自相矛盾：比如，她只肯每周给厨娘一两次机会，让她不受拘束地与亲人来往——而这种机会，雇主自己一家人凭着家庭诉求是时时刻刻都在享有的。这本身就反民主，因为它区分了家庭生活对一群人的价值与对另一群人的价值；或者毋宁说，它断言一群人远不如另一群人重要，以致属于前者的生命中宝贵的一面，就该为后者牺牲。

===Original===
This cannot be defended theoretically, and no doubt much of the talk among the employers of household labor, that their employees are carefully shielded and cared for, and that it is so much better for a girl’s health and morals to work in a household than to work in a factory, comes from a certain uneasiness of conscience, and from a desire to make up by individual scruple what would be done much more freely and naturally by public opinion if it had an untrammelled chance to assert itself. One person, or a number of isolated persons, however conscientious, cannot perform this office of public opinion. Certain hospitals in London have contributed statistics showing that seventy-eight percent of illegitimate children born there are the children of girls working in households. These girls are certainly not less virtuous than factory girls, for they come from the same families and have had the same training, but the girls who remain at home and work in factories meet their lovers naturally and easily, their fathers and brothers know the men, and unconsciously exercise a certain supervision and a certain direction in their choice of companionship. The household employees living in another part of the city, away from their natural family and social ties, depend upon chance for the lovers whom they meet. The lover may be the young man who delivers for the butcher or grocer, or the solitary friend, who follows the girl from her own part of town and pursues unfairly the advantage which her social loneliness and isolation afford him. There is no available public opinion nor any standard of convention which the girl can apply to her own situation.

===Chinese===
这在理论上无从辩护；而家务雇主们那一套言说——说她们的雇工被小心翼翼地庇护着、照料着，说姑娘在人家做工比在工厂做工对健康与道德好得多——多半出自某种良心不安，出自一种愿望：想以个人的谨小慎微，去补偿倘若公众舆论有机会不受拘束地申张、本会自由得多也自然得多地去做的事。一个人，或一批孤立的人，无论多么尽责，都无法履行公众舆论的这一职能。伦敦一些医院提供的统计表明，在那里出生的私生子里，百分之七十八是在人家做工的姑娘生的。这些姑娘肯定不比工厂女工更不贞洁，因为她们出自同样的家庭、受过同样的教养；只是留在家里、在工厂做工的姑娘，能够自然轻易地与情人相会，她们的父亲兄弟认识那些男人，并在不知不觉中对她们择伴的事情行使着某种监督、某种指引。而住在城市另一头、远离天然家庭与社会纽带的家务劳动者，遇见什么样的情人全凭运气。那情人可能是替肉铺或杂货店送货的年轻人，也可能是独一个的朋友，从姑娘自己住的街区跟了来，不公道地追逐她的社交孤独与隔绝给他造成的便宜。姑娘身边没有可用的公众舆论，也没有任何习俗标准，可以拿来衡量她自己的处境。

===Original===
It would be easy to point out many inconveniences arising from the fact that the old economic forms are retained when moral conditions which befitted them have entirely disappeared, but until employers of domestic labor become conscious of their narrow code of ethics, and make a distinct effort to break through the status of mistress and servant, because it shocks their moral sense, there is no chance of even beginning a reform.

===Chinese===
旧的经济形式被保留下来，而与它相称的道德境况却已完全消失——由此产生的种种不便，要一一指出并不难；可是，除非家务雇主们意识到自己那套狭隘的伦理法典，并因主仆名分触犯她们的道德感而痛下决心冲破它，否则连开始一场改革的机会都没有。

===Original===
A fuller social and domestic life among household employees would be steps toward securing their entrance into the larger industrial organizations by which the needs of a community are most successfully administered. Many a girl who complains of loneliness, and who relinquishes her situation with that as her sole excuse, feebly tries to formulate her sense of restraint and social maladjustment. She sometimes says that she “feels so unnatural all the time.” The writer has known the voice of a girl to change so much during three weeks of “service” that she could not recognize it when the girl returned to her home. It alternated between the high falsetto in which a shy child “speaks a piece” and the husky gulp with which the globus hystericus is swallowed. The alertness and bonhomie of the voice of the tenement-house child had totally disappeared. When such a girl leaves her employer, her reasons are often incoherent and totally incomprehensible to that good lady, who naturally concludes that she wishes to get away from the work and back to her dances and giddy life, content, if she has these, to stand many hours in an insanitary factory. The charge of the employer is only half a truth. These dances may be the only organized form of social life which the disheartened employee is able to mention, but the girl herself, in her discontent and her moving from place to place, is blindly striving to respond to a larger social life. Her employer thinks that she should be able to consider only the interests and conveniences of her employer’s family, because the employer herself is holding to a family outlook, and refuses to allow her mind to take in the larger aspects of the situation.

===Chinese===
家务劳动者中间更充实的社交与家庭生活，将是通向若干步骤的起点，使他们得以进入更宏大的产业组织——社区的需要正是借助那些组织才得到最妥善的照管。许多姑娘抱怨孤独，并以此为唯一的借口辞去差事；她们是在微弱地试图说出自己所感到的拘束与社交失调。她有时说，自己「总觉得不自然」。作者曾见过一个姑娘仅「帮佣」三星期，嗓音就变得她回到家时作者都认不出来：一会儿是害羞的孩子「背诵一段诗文」时那种尖细的假嗓，一会儿是咽下癔症球（globus hystericus）时那种嘶哑的吞咽。廉租公寓里孩子那副机敏快活的嗓音，消失得无影无踪。这样的姑娘离开雇主时，理由常常语无伦次，令那位好太太全然无法理解；太太自然断定，她是想躲开活计，回到她的舞会与轻狂生活中去——只要能有这些，哪怕在卫生恶劣的工厂里一站许多个钟头，她也心甘。雇主的指控只有一半是实情。这些舞会，也许只是心灰的雇工所能说出的唯一有组织的社交生活形式；可姑娘本人，在她的不满与东家西家地流转之中，正盲目地努力回应一种更广阔的社会生活。她的雇主认为，她理应只考虑雇主一家的利益与便利，因为雇主自己固守着家庭的视界，拒绝让自己的心智去容纳处境更宏大的方方面面。

===Original===
Although this household industry survives in the midst of the factory system, it must, of course, constantly compete with it. Women with little children, or those with invalids depending upon them, cannot enter either occupation, and they are practically confined to the sewing trades; but to all other untrained women seeking employment a choice is open between these two forms of labor.

===Chinese===
这一家庭工业虽在工厂制度的环绕之中存续，当然也就不得不时时与之竞争。带着幼童的女人，或需照看病弱之人的女人，两种行当都进不得，实际上只能限于缝纫一行；但对其他所有无专技而求职的女人来说，这两种劳动形式之间的选择是敞开的。

===Original===
There are few women so dull that they cannot paste labels on a box, or do some form of factory work; few so dull that some perplexed housekeeper will not receive them, at least for a trial, in her household. Household labor, then, has to compete with factory labor, and women seeking employment, more or less consciously compare these two forms of labor in point of hours, in point of permanency of employment, in point of wages, and in point of the advantage they afford for family and social life. Three points are easily disposed of. First, in regard to hours, there is no doubt that the factory has the advantage. The average factory hours are from seven in the morning to six in the evening, with the chance of working overtime in busy seasons. This leaves most of the evenings and Sundays entirely free. The average hours of household labor are from six in the morning until eight at night, with little difference in seasons. There is one afternoon a week, with an occasional evening, but Sunday is seldom wholly free. Even these evenings and afternoons take the form of a concession from the employer. They are called “evenings out,” as if the time really belonged to her, but that she was graciously permitting her employee to use it. This attitude, of course, is in marked contrast to that maintained by the factory operative, who, when she works evenings is paid for “overtime.”

===Chinese===
很少有女人迟钝到不会往盒子上贴标签、干不了某种工厂活计；也很少有女人迟钝到没有哪位惶惑的主妇肯收留她——至少试上一试。于是，家务劳动不得不与工厂劳动竞争；求职的女人们或多或少自觉地就四点比较这两种劳动：工时、雇用的稳定、工资，以及它们给家庭生活与社交生活带来的好处。其中三点容易说清。第一，就工时而言，工厂无疑占优。工厂平均工时是早晨七点到傍晚六点，旺季还有加班的机会。这样，大多数晚间与星期日便完全空了出来。家务劳动的平均工时是早晨六点到晚上八点，季节之间几乎没什么差别。每周一个下午，偶尔一个晚上，而星期日难得完全空出。就连这些晚上与下午，也采取着雇主恩赐的形式。它们被称作「外出晚」（"evenings out"），仿佛时间本属雇主所有，只是她开恩，准许雇工去用一用。这种态度，与工厂女工所持的态度恰成对照：她们晚上做工，得到的是「加班」的报酬。

===Original===
Second, in regard to permanency of position, the advantage is found clearly on the side of the household employee, if she proves in any measure satisfactory to her employer, for she encounters much less competition.

===Chinese===
第二，就职位的稳定而言，好处显然在家务劳动者一边：只要她在某种程度上令雇主满意，她遇到的竞争便少得多。

===Original===
Third, in point of wages, the household is again fairly ahead, if we consider not the money received, but the opportunity offered for saving money. This is greater among household employees, because they do not pay board, the clothing required is simpler, and the temptation to spend money in recreation is less frequent. The minimum wages paid an adult in household labor may be fairly put at two dollars and a half a week; the maximum at six dollars, this excluding the comparatively rare opportunities for women to cook at forty dollars a month, and the housekeeper’s position at fifty dollars a month.

===Chinese===
第三，就工资而言，倘若我们看的不是到手的钱，而是省钱的机会，家务这行也还略占上风。家务劳动者省钱的机会更大，因为她们不必付膳宿费，所需的衣着更简单，把钱花在娱乐上的诱惑也更少。成年家务劳动者的最低工资，大体可定为每周两美元半；最高六美元——这还没有算上女人当厨娘月俸四十美元、当女管家月俸五十美元那些比较难得的机会。

===Original===
The factory wages, viewed from the savings-bank point of view, may be smaller in the average, but this is doubtless counterbalanced in the minds of the employees by the greater chance which the factory offers for increased wages. A girl over sixteen seldom works in a factory for less than four dollars a week, and always cherishes the hope of at last being a forewoman with a permanent salary of from fifteen to twenty-five dollars a week. Whether she attains this or not, she runs a fair chance of earning ten dollars a week as a skilled worker. A girl finds it easier to be content with three dollars a week, when she pays for board, in a scale of wages rising toward ten dollars, than to be content with four dollars a week and pay no board, in a scale of wages rising toward six dollars; and the girl well knows that there are scores of forewomen at sixty dollars a month for one forty-dollar cook or fifty-dollar housekeeper. In many cases this position is well taken economically, for, although the opportunity for saving may be better for the employees in the household than in the factory, her family saves more when she works in a factory and lives with them. The rent is no more when she is at home. The two dollars and a half a week which she pays into the family fund more than covers the cost of her actual food, and at night she can often contribute toward the family labor by helping her mother wash and sew.

===Chinese===
从储蓄银行的视角看，工厂的平均工资或许较低，但在雇工们心里，这无疑被工厂提供的更大概率的长薪机会抵消了。十六岁以上的姑娘在工厂做工，很少低于每周四美元，而且总怀着希望：有朝一日当上女领班，拿每周十五到二十五美元的固定薪水。无论是否当得上，她都很有机会作为熟练工人挣到每周十美元。一个姑娘会发现：在一份朝十美元攀升的工资序列里自付膳宿、周挣三美元，比在一份封顶六美元的工资序列里不付膳宿、周挣四美元，更容易心安；况且姑娘心里清楚，每有一个月俸四十美元的厨娘或五十美元的女管家，就有几十个月俸六十美元的女领班。就经济而言，她这盘算在许多情形下是站得住的：尽管在人家做工，省钱的机会或许更好，可当她在工厂做工、与家人同住时，她的家省得更多。她住在家里，房租分文不多；她每周缴进家庭公账的两美元半，远不止抵她实际的饭食开销；到了晚上，她还常常帮母亲洗衣缝补，为家庭劳动出一把力。

===Original===
The fourth point has already been considered, and if the premise in regard to the isolation of the household employee is well taken, and if the position can be sustained that this isolation proves the determining factor in the situation, then certainly an effort should be made to remedy this, at least in its domestic and social aspects. To allow household employees to live with their own families and among their own friends, as factory employees now do, would be to relegate more production to industrial centres administered on the factory system, and to secure shorter hours for that which remains to be done in the household.

===Chinese===
第四点前面已经讨论过。倘若关于家务劳动者孤立的那个前提站得住，倘若能够立论说，这孤立正是整个处境中的决定性因素，那么，人们当然应当努力补救这一点，至少补救其家庭与社交的方面。允许家务劳动者像如今的工厂雇工一样，住在自己家人中间、自己朋友中间，将意味着把更多的生产交给按工厂制度管理的产业中心，并为留在家庭里去做的那些活计争得更短的工时。

===Original===
In those cases in which the household employees have no family ties, doubtless a remedy against social isolation would be the formation of residence clubs, at least in the suburbs, where the isolation is most keenly felt. Indeed, the beginnings of these clubs are already seen in the servants’ quarters at the summer hotels. In these residence clubs, the household employee could have the independent life which only one’s own abiding place can afford. This, of course, presupposes a higher grade of ability than household employees at present possess; on the other hand, it is only by offering such possibilities that the higher grades of intelligence can be secured for household employment. As the plan of separate clubs for household employees will probably come first in the suburbs, where the difficulty of securing and holding “servants” under the present system is most keenly felt, so the plan of buying cooked food from an outside kitchen, and of having more and more of the household product relegated to the factory, will probably come from the comparatively poor people in the city, who feel most keenly the pressure of the present system. They already consume a much larger proportion of canned goods and bakers’ wares and “prepared meats” than the more prosperous people do, because they cannot command the skill nor the time for the more tedious preparation of the raw material. The writer has seen a tenement-house mother pass by a basket of green peas at the door of a local grocery store, to purchase a tin of canned peas, because they could be easily prepared for supper and “the children liked the tinny taste.”

===Chinese===
在家务劳动者没有家庭纽带的那些情形里，对抗社交孤立的良方，无疑是组建寄宿俱乐部（residence clubs），至少先在城郊——那里的孤立感受最切。其实，这些俱乐部的雏形，已在避暑旅馆的仆役宿舍里初露端倪。在这样的寄宿俱乐部里，家务劳动者便能过上独立的生活——那种唯有自己的居所才能给予的独立生活。这当然以高于家务劳动者现有水准的才具为前提；可反过来说，也只有提供这样的可能性，才能为家务雇用吸引到更高水准的智力。正如为家务劳动者另建俱乐部的方案多半会先在城郊实行——在现行制度下招聘并留住「仆人」的困难，在那里感受最切——向外部厨房购买熟食、把越来越多的家庭产品交给工厂的方案，也多半会来自城里较穷的人们，他们对现行制度的压力感受最深。他们消费的罐头食品、面包房成品与「预制肉食」，比例已经比富裕人家大得多，因为他们既无手艺、也无时间去作那些更费工夫的原料备办。作者见过一位廉租公寓的母亲，在本地杂货店门口对一篮鲜豌豆视而不见，却买了一听豌豆罐头，因为罐头做晚饭省事，而且「孩子们喜欢那股马口铁味儿」。

===Original===
It is comparatively easy for an employer to manage her household industry with a cook, a laundress, a waitress. The difficulties really begin when the family income is so small that but one person can be employed in the household for all these varied functions, and the difficulties increase and grow almost insurmountable as they fall altogether upon the mother of the family, who is living in a flat, or, worse still, in a tenement house, where one stove and one set of utensils must be put to all sorts of uses, fit or unfit, making the living room of the family a horror in summer, and perfectly insupportable on rainy washing-days in winter. Such a woman, rather than the prosperous housekeeper, uses factory products, and thus no high standard of quality is established.

===Chinese===
一位雇主雇上厨娘、洗衣妇、侍餐女仆来打理她的家庭工业，还比较容易。真正的困难始于家庭收入小到只能雇一个人来包揽这许多不同的职能；而当这些职能整个落到那位母亲身上时——她住在一套公寓里，或者更糟，住在一幢廉租公寓里，一只炉子、一套器具必须派上种种用场，合式的或不合式的——困难便节节增长，几乎不可逾越：夏天，家里的起居室成了苦处；冬天逢着落雨的洗衣日，更全然无法忍受。正是这样的女人，而不是富裕的主妇，在使用工厂的产品；于是，高品质的标准便无从确立。

===Original===
The problem of domestic service, which has long been discussed in the United States and England, is now coming to prominence in France. As a well-known economist has recently pointed out, the large defection in the ranks of domestics is there regarded as a sign of revolt against an “unconscious slavery,” while English and American writers appeal to the statistics which point to the absorption of an enormous number of the class from which servants were formerly recruited into factory employments, and urge, as the natural solution, that more of the products used in households be manufactured in factories, and that personal service, at least for healthy adults, be eliminated altogether. Both of these lines of discussion certainly indicate that domestic service is yielding to the influence of a democratic movement, and is emerging from the narrower code of family ethics into the larger code governing social relations. It still remains to express the ethical advance through changed economic conditions by which the actual needs of the family may be supplied not only more effectively but more in line with associated effort. To fail to apprehend the tendency of one’s age, and to fail to adapt the conditions of an industry to it, is to leave that industry ill-adjusted and belated on the economic side, and out of line ethically.

===Chinese===
家务服务（domestic service）的问题在美国与英国久经讨论，如今正在法国崭露头角。正如一位著名经济学家最近指出的，那边把家仆队伍的大批流失，视为反抗「无意识的奴役」（"unconscious slavery"）的征兆；而英美作家则诉诸统计——统计表明，从前提供仆人的那个阶级，已有巨量人口被工厂雇用吸收——并把更多的家用产品改由工厂制造、把人身侍奉至少就健康的成年人而言全然废除，作为顺理成章的解决方案加以力陈。这两种论调无疑都表明：家务服务正在向民主运动的影响让步，正从较狭隘的家庭伦理法典中走出，进入支配社会关系的更宏大的法典。尚待完成的是：通过改变经济条件来表达这一伦理的进步，使家庭的实际需要不仅得到更有效的供给，而且更合乎联合的努力。不能把握自己时代的趋向，不能使一种产业的条件与之相适应，便是让那产业在经济上失调、滞后，在伦理上脱节。

## V Industrial Amelioration / 产业改良

===Original===
There is no doubt that the great difficulty we experience in reducing to action our imperfect code of social ethics arises from the fact that we have not yet learned to act together, and find it far from easy even to fuse our principles and aims into a satisfactory statement. We have all been at times entertained by the futile efforts of half a dozen highly individualized people gathered together as a committee. Their aimless attempts to find a common method of action have recalled the wavering motion of a baby’s arm before he has learned to coordinate his muscles.

===Chinese===
毫无疑问，我们把自己那套尚不完善的社会伦理法典付诸行动时所感到的巨大困难，源于这样一桩事实：我们还不曾学会共同行动，甚至要把我们的原则与目标熔铸成一份令人满意的表述，也远非易事。六个个人色彩极浓的人聚成一个委员会，那副徒劳的努力，我们都不时引为笑谈。他们漫无目的地试图寻找一种共同的行动方法，令人想起婴儿在学会协调肌肉之前手臂的那阵摇摆。

===Original===
If, as is many times stated, we are passing from an age of individualism to one of association, there is no doubt that for decisive and effective action the individual still has the best of it. He will secure efficient results while committees are still deliberating upon the best method of making a beginning. And yet, if the need of the times demand associated effort, it may easily be true that the action which appears ineffective, and yet is carried out upon the more highly developed line of associated effort, may represent a finer social quality and have a greater social value than the more effective individual action. It is possible that an individual may be successful, largely because he conserves all his powers for individual achievement and does not put any of his energy into the training which will give him the ability to act with others. The individual acts promptly, and we are dazzled by his success while only dimly conscious of the inadequacy of his code. Nowhere is this illustrated more clearly than in industrial relations, as existing between the owner of a large factory and his employees.

===Chinese===
倘若如人们多次申言的那样，我们正从个人主义的时代过渡到联合的时代，那么毫无疑问，就果断而有效的行动而言，个人仍占尽上风。当各个委员会还在斟酌着以哪种方式开头为好时，他已经拿到了高效的成果。然而，倘若时代的需要确乎要求联合努力（associated effort），那么下面这一点很可能是真的：一种看似收效甚微、却是循着联合努力这条更发达的路线执行的行动，可能代表着一种更精微的社会品质，具有比更有效的个人行动更大的社会价值。一个人之所以可能成功，很可能是因为他把全部力量都留作个人成就之用，而不肯把任何精力投入那种将赋予他与他人协同行动之能力的训练。个人行动迅捷，他的成功使我们目眩，而我们对他那套法典的不足，却只有朦胧的意识。再没有比产业关系——大工厂主与其雇工之间的关系——更能清楚说明这一点的了。

===Original===
A growing conflict may be detected between the democratic ideal, which urges the workmen to demand representation in the administration of industry, and the accepted position, that the man who owns the capital and takes the risks has the exclusive right of management. It is in reality a clash between individual or aristocratic management, and corporate or democratic management. A large and highly developed factory presents a sharp contrast between its socialized form and individualistic ends.

===Chinese===
人们可以察觉到一场日益增长的冲突：一端是民主理想，它敦促工人要求在产业管理中获得代表权；另一端是公认的立场，即拥有资本、承担风险的人握有排他的管理权。这实际上是个人式或贵族式的管理，与社团式或民主式的管理之间的碰撞。一座庞大而高度发达的工厂，在其社会化的形式与个人主义的目的之间，呈现出尖锐的反差。

===Original===
It is possible to illustrate this difference by a series of events which occurred in Chicago during the summer of 1894. These events epitomized and exaggerated, but at the same time challenged, the code of ethics which regulates much of our daily conduct, and clearly showed that so-called social relations are often resting upon the will of an individual, and are in reality regulated by a code of individual ethics.

===Chinese===
1894 年夏发生在芝加哥的一系列事件，可以把这种差别说明得很清楚。这些事件把支配我们日常行为大半的那套伦理法典凝聚呈现出来并加以放大，同时又对它提出了挑战；它们清楚地表明：所谓社会关系，往往建立在一个人的意志之上，实际上受着一套个人伦理法典的支配。

===Original===
As this situation illustrates a point of great difficulty to which we have arrived in our development of social ethics, it may be justifiable to discuss it at some length. Let us recall the facts, not as they have been investigated and printed, but as they remain in our memories.

===Chinese===
既然这一局面说明的是我们在社会伦理的发展进程中所抵达的一个重大难点，把它稍加详尽地讨论一番，或许是有道理的。让我们回顾那些事实吧——不是经过调查、付诸刊印的事实，而是留存在我们记忆中的事实。

===Original===
A large manufacturing company had provided commodious workshops, and, at the instigation of its president, had built a model town for the use of its employees. After a series of years it was deemed necessary, during a financial depression, to reduce the wages of these employees by giving each workman less than full-time work “in order to keep the shops open.” This reduction was not accepted by the men, who had become discontented with the factory management and the town regulations, and a strike ensued, followed by a complete shutdown of the works. Although these shops were nonunion shops, the strikers were hastily organized and appealed for help to the American Railway Union, which at that moment was holding its biennial meeting in Chicago. After some days’ discussion and some futile attempts at arbitration, a sympathetic strike was declared, which gradually involved railway men in all parts of the country, and orderly transportation was brought to a complete standstill. In the excitement which followed, cars were burned and tracks torn up. The police of Chicago did not cope with the disorder, and the railway companies, apparently distrusting the Governor of the State, and in order to protect the United States mails, called upon the President of the United States for the federal troops, the federal courts further enjoined all persons against any form of interference with the property or operation of the railroads, and the situation gradually assumed the proportions of internecine warfare. During all of these events the president of the manufacturing company first involved, steadfastly refused to have the situation submitted to arbitration, and this attitude naturally provoked much discussion. The discussion was broadly divided between those who held that the long kindness of the president of the company had been most ungratefully received, and those who maintained that the situation was the inevitable outcome of the social consciousness developing among working people. The first defended the president of the company in his persistent refusal to arbitrate, maintaining that arbitration was impossible after the matter had been taken up by other than his own employees, and they declared that a man must be allowed to run his own business. They considered the firm stand of the president a service to the manufacturing interests of the entire country. The others claimed that a large manufacturing concern has ceased to be a private matter; that not only a number of workmen and stockholders are concerned in its management, but that the interests of the public are so involved that the officers of the company are in a real sense administering a public trust.

===Chinese===
一家大型制造公司备下了宽敞的厂房，又在其总裁的倡议下建起一座模范城镇（model town），供其雇工居住。过了若干年，时值一场金融萧条，公司认为有必要削减这些雇工的工资，办法是让每个工人做不足全工时的活计，「以便维持开工」。工人们不接受这一削减——他们早已对工厂的管理和城镇的规章心怀不满——于是罢工随之而起，接着是工厂的全面停业。这些工厂虽是非工会工厂（nonunion shop），罢工者还是匆忙组织起来，向美国铁路工会（American Railway Union）求援；那一刻，该工会正在芝加哥举行两年一度的大会。经过几天的讨论和几次徒劳的仲裁尝试，一场同情罢工（sympathetic strike）宣告发动，逐渐把全国各地的铁路员工卷了进来，井然的运输陷于完全的停摆。随之而来的骚乱中，车厢被焚毁，路轨被撬翻。芝加哥的警察对付不了这场乱局；铁路公司显然信不过本州州长，又为保护美国的邮政，向美国总统请调联邦军队；联邦法院进而禁止任何人以任何形式妨害铁路的财产或运营；局势渐渐具有了自相残杀之战事的规模。在所有这些事件当中，最初牵涉其中的那家制造公司的总裁，始终拒不把局势交付仲裁，这种态度自然激起了大量讨论。讨论大致分为两派：一派认为，公司总裁多年的厚遇遭到了最忘恩负义的报答；另一派则主张，这一局面是劳动人民中间正在发展的社会意识的必然结果。前者为公司总裁拒不仲裁的顽固立场辩护，坚称事态既已由他自家雇工以外的人接手，仲裁便无从谈起；他们宣称，必须允许一个人经营自己的生意。他们把总裁的坚定立场视为对全国制造业利益的一项贡献。后者则声称，一家大型制造企业已不再是一桩私事；不仅大批工人与股东关切着它的管理，公众的利益也牵涉如此之深，以致公司的主管人员在一种真正的意义上是在管理着一份公众信托（public trust）。

===Original===
This prolonged strike clearly puts in a concrete form the ethics of an individual, in this case a benevolent employer, and the ethics of a mass of men, his employees, claiming what they believed to be their moral rights.

===Chinese===
这场旷日持久的罢工，把一个人的伦理——在这个例子中是一位仁厚的雇主——与一群人即他的雇工的伦理，清晰地铸成了具体的形式；后者所申张的，是他们相信属于自己的道德权利。

===Original===
These events illustrate the difficulty of managing an industry which has become organized into a vast social operation, not with the cooperation of the workman thus socialized, but solely by the dictation of the individual owning the capital. There is a sharp divergence between the social form and the individual aim, which becomes greater as the employees are more highly socialized and dependent. The president of the company under discussion went further than the usual employer does. He socialized not only the factory, but the form in which his workmen were living. He built, and in a great measure regulated, an entire town, without calling upon the workmen either for self-expression or self-government. He honestly believed that he knew better than they what was for their good, as he certainly knew better than they how to conduct his business. As his factory developed and increased, making money each year under his direction, he naturally expected the town to prosper in the same way.

===Chinese===
这些事件说明：一门已经组织成庞大社会运作的产业，不借助由此社会化了工人的合作，只凭拥有资本的个人的独断来管理，是何等困难。社会化的形式与个人的目的之间存在尖锐的分歧，而雇工们的社会化程度愈高、依附性愈强，这分歧就愈大。我们所讨论的这位公司总裁，比寻常的雇主走得更远：他不仅使工厂社会化，连他工人们的生活方式也一并社会化了。他建起并在很大程度上支配着一整座城镇，却从不要求工人们自我表达、自我治理。他诚恳地相信，什么对他们有好处，他比他们自己更清楚，正如怎样经营他的生意，他也确实比他们更清楚。随着他的工厂日渐发展壮大、在他的主持下年年赢利，他自然指望这座城镇也以同样的方式繁荣起来。

===Original===
He did not realize that the men submitted to the undemocratic conditions of the factory organization because the economic pressure in our industrial affairs is so great that they could not do otherwise. Under this pressure they could be successfully discouraged from organization, and systematically treated on the individual basis.

===Chinese===
他没有意识到，工人们之所以屈从于工厂组织中那种反民主的条件，是因为我们产业事务中的经济压力太大，他们别无选择。在这种压力之下，他们可以被成功地阻止组织起来，被按个人的基准系统地对待。

===Original===
Social life, however, in spite of class distinctions, is much freer than industrial life, and the men resented the extension of industrial control to domestic and social arrangements. They felt the lack of democracy in the assumption that they should be taken care of in these matters, in which even the humblest workman has won his independence. The basic difficulty lay in the fact that an individual was directing the social affairs of many men without any consistent effort to find out their desires, and without any organization through which to give them social expression. The president of the company was, moreover, so confident of the righteousness of his aim that he had come to test the righteousness of the process by his own feelings and not by those of the men. He doubtless built the town from a sincere desire to give his employees the best surroundings. As it developed, he gradually took toward it the artist attitude toward his own creation, which has no thought for the creation itself but is absorbed in the idea it stands for, and he ceased to measure the usefulness of the town by the standard of the men’s needs. This process slowly darkened his glints of memory, which might have connected his experience with that of his men. It is possible to cultivate the impulses of the benefactor until the power of attaining a simple human relationship with the beneficiaries, that of frank equality with them, is gone, and there is left no mutual interest in a common cause. To perform too many good deeds may be to lose the power of recognizing good in others; to be too absorbed in carrying out a personal plan of improvement may be to fail to catch the great moral lesson which our times offer.

===Chinese===
然而，社会生活尽管有种种阶级区分，仍比产业生活自由得多；工人们怨恨产业控制向家务与社交安排的延伸。在这些事情上，竟认定他们该由别人照管——而在这些事情上，连最卑微的工人也已赢得了独立——他们感到了其中民主的欠缺。根本的困难在于：一个人指挥着许多人的社会事务，却从不持续下功夫去探明他们的愿望，也没有任何组织可以让工人们作社会的表达。况且，这位公司总裁对自己的目的之正当如此自信，以致他渐渐只用他自己的感受、而不用工人们的感受，来检验过程的正当与否。毫无疑问，他建这座城镇，出自让雇工得到最好环境的诚挚愿望。随着城镇的发展，他对它渐渐摆出了艺术家对自己的作品的那种态度——对作品本身全无体恤，全神贯注的只是它所代表的观念——他不再用工人需要的标准来衡量这座城镇的用处。这一过程慢慢暗淡了他记忆的微光，那微光本可以把他的经验与工人们的经验连在一起。施惠者（benefactor）的冲动是可以被培育到这般地步的：与受惠者（beneficiary）达成一种单纯的人际关系、一种与他们坦率平等的关系的能力，已然丧失，对一项共同事业的相互关切也就荡然无存。善事做得太多，可能会失去辨识他人之善的能力；太专注于推行一项个人的改良计划，可能会错过我们时代给予的那个伟大的道德教训。

===Original===
The president of this company fostered his employees for many years; he gave them sanitary houses and beautiful parks; but in their extreme need, when they were struggling with the most difficult situation which the times could present to them, he lost his touch and had nothing wherewith to help them. The employer’s conception of goodness for his men had been cleanliness, decency of living, and, above all, thrift and temperance. Means had been provided for all this, and opportunities had also been given for recreation and improvement. But this employer suddenly found his town in the sweep of a worldwide moral impulse. A movement had been going on about him and among his working men, of which he had been unconscious, or concerning which he had heard only by rumor.

===Chinese===
这位公司总裁养育他的雇工多年；他给了他们卫生的住宅和美丽的公园；可在他们极度的困厄中，当他们与时代所能呈现的最艰难处境搏斗时，他却失了与之相通的触感，拿不出任何可以帮助他们的东西。这位雇主对他工人们的「善」的观念，一向是清洁、体面的生活，尤其是节俭与节制。为这一切，手段都已备齐；消遣与进益的机会也已给出。可这位雇主忽然发现，他的城镇正被一场世界性的道德冲动（moral impulse）席卷。一场运动早就在他周围、在他的工人们中间进行着，而他对它毫无知觉，或仅只风闻。

===Original===
Outside the ken of philanthropists the proletariat had learned to say in many languages, that “the injury of one is the concern of all.” Their watchwords were brotherhood, sacrifice, the subordination of individual and trade interests, to the good of the working classes, and they were moved by a determination to free that class from the untoward conditions under which they were laboring.

===Chinese===
在慈善家们的视界之外，无产阶级已经学会用许多种语言说：「一人所受的损害，即是全体所关切的事。」他们的口号是兄弟情谊、牺牲、使个人利益与行业利益服从于工人阶级的福祉；推动他们的，是一种决心——要把这个阶级从他们劳作于其中的种种不利境况中解放出来。

===Original===
Compared to these watchwords, the old ones which this philanthropic employer had given his town were negative and inadequate. He had believed strongly in temperance and steadiness of individual effort, but had failed to apprehend the greater movement of combined abstinence and concerted action. With all his fostering, the president had not attained to a conception of social morality for his men and had imagined that virtue for them largely meant absence of vice.

===Chinese===
与这些口号相比，这位慈善家雇主给他城镇立下的旧口号，便显得消极而不完备。他曾笃信节制，笃信个人努力的平稳持恒，却未能领会联合节制与协同行动这一更宏大的运动。养育尽管养育，这位总裁始终不曾为他的人们达到一种社会道德（social morality）的观念，还想象对他们来说，德性大抵就意味着没有恶行。

===Original===
When the labor movement finally stirred his town, or, to speak more fairly, when, in their distress and perplexity, his own employees appealed to an organized manifestation of this movement, they were quite sure that simply because they were workmen in distress they would not be deserted by it. This loyalty on the part of a widely ramified and well-organized union toward the workmen in a “nonunion shop,” who had contributed nothing to its cause, was certainly a manifestation of moral power.

===Chinese===
当劳工运动终于撼动他的城镇时——或者更公道地说，当他的雇工们在困厄与惶惑中求告于这场运动的一个有组织的显现时——他们十分笃定：单单因为他们是陷于困厄的工人，这场运动便不会抛弃他们。一个枝脉广布、组织良好的工会，对「非工会工厂」里那些从未为其事业出过一分力的工人竟如此忠诚，这无疑是道德力量的一场显现。

===Original===
In none of his utterances or correspondence did the president for an instant recognize this touch of nobility, although one would imagine that he would gladly point out this bit of virtue, in what he must have considered the moral ruin about him. He stood throughout for the individual virtues, those which had distinguished the model workmen of his youth; those which had enabled him and so many of his contemporaries to rise in life, when “rising in life” was urged upon every promising boy as the goal of his efforts.

===Chinese===
在这位总裁的言论与通信里，他一刻也不曾认出这丝高贵；尽管人们会想，在他必定视为四周道德废墟的地方，他本该乐于指出这一点点德行。他自始至终站在个人美德一边——那是他青年时代模范工人的美德，是使他和他那一代许许多多人得以出人头地的美德；在那些岁月里，「出人头地」被当作目标，敦促着每一个有出息的男孩去努力。

===Original===
Of the code of social ethics he had caught absolutely nothing. The morals he had advocated in selecting and training his men did not fail them in the hour of confusion. They were self-controlled, and they themselves destroyed no property. They were sober and exhibited no drunkenness, even although obliged to hold their meetings in the saloon hall of a neighboring town. They repaid their employer in kind, but he had given them no rule for the life of association into which they were plunged.

===Chinese===
至于社会伦理的法典，他则全然一无领会。他在挑选和训练工人时所倡导的那些德目，在惶惑的时刻也没有辜负他们。他们克制，自己没有毁坏任何财产。他们清醒，没有露出半点醉态，尽管不得不借邻镇酒馆的大厅开会。他们以同样的德报答了雇主；可对于他们一头扎进的那种联合生活，雇主却不曾给过他们任何规条。

===Original===
The president of the company desired that his employees should possess the individual and family virtues, but did nothing to cherish in them the social virtues which express themselves in associated effort.

===Chinese===
这位公司总裁指望他的雇工具备个人的与家庭的美德，却不曾做任何事情，去珍护他们心中那在联合努力中表达自己的社会美德。

===Original===
Day after day, during that horrible time of suspense, when the wires constantly reported the same message, “the President of the Company holds that there is nothing to arbitrate,” one was forced to feel that the ideal of one-man rule was being sustained in its baldest form. A demand from many parts of the country and from many people was being made for social adjustment, against which the commercial training and the individualistic point of view held its own successfully.

===Chinese===
在那段可怕的悬望岁月里，电报一日复一日传来同一个消息：「公司总裁坚持没有什么可仲裁的。」人不得不感到，「一人统治」（one-man rule）的理想正以它最赤裸的形式得到维护。来自全国许多地方、许多人的社会调适的要求被提了出来，而商业的训练与个人主义的观点，则成功地守住了自己的阵地。

===Original===
The majority of the stockholders, not only of this company but of similar companies, and many other citizens, who had had the same commercial experience, shared and sustained this position. It was quite impossible for them to catch the other point of view. They not only felt themselves right from the commercial standpoint, but had gradually accustomed themselves also to the philanthropic standpoint, until they had come to consider their motives beyond reproach. Habit held them persistent in this view of the case through all changing conditions.

===Chinese===
不仅这家公司、连同类公司的多数股东，以及许许多多有着同样商业经验的其他公民，都分享并坚持着这一立场。要他们抓住另一种观点，是全然不可能的。他们不仅从商业的立场上自觉有理，还渐渐习惯了慈善家的立场，直到把自己的动机看作无可指摘。习惯使他们在此事上固执己见，任凭时局如何变迁。

===Original===
A wise man has said that “the consent of men and your own conscience are two wings given you whereby you may rise to God.” It is so easy for the good and powerful to think that they can rise by following the dictates of conscience, by pursuing their own ideals, that they are prone to leave those ideals unconnected with the consent of their fellow-men. The president of the company thought out within his own mind a beautiful town. He had power with which to build this town, but he did not appeal to nor obtain the consent of the men who were living in it. The most unambitious reform, recognizing the necessity for this consent, makes for slow but sane and strenuous progress, while the most ambitious of social plans and experiments, ignoring this, is prone to failure.

===Chinese===
一位智者说过：「众人的认可与你自己的良心，是赋予你的两只翅膀，你可借此升向上帝。」善良而有权势的人太容易以为，只消听从良心的指使、追求自己的理想，便能飞升，于是他们往往任凭那些理想与同胞的认可两不相干。这位公司总裁在他自己的心里设想出一座美丽的城镇。他有建起这座城镇的权势，却不去恳求、也没有得到住在里面的工人们的认可。最不野心勃勃的改革，因为承认这种认可的必要，走得慢，却走得健全而奋发；而最野心勃勃的社会计划与社会实验，一旦忽视这一点，便容易失败。

===Original===
The man who insists upon consent, who moves with the people, is bound to consult the “feasible right” as well as the absolute right. He is often obliged to attain only Mr. Lincoln’s “best possible,” and then has the sickening sense of compromise with his best convictions. He has to move along with those whom he leads toward a goal that neither he nor they see very clearly till they come to it. He has to discover what people really want, and then “provide the channels in which the growing moral force of their lives shall flow.” What he does attain, however, is not the result of his individual striving, as a solitary mountain-climber beyond that of the valley multitude but it is sustained and upheld by the sentiments and aspirations of many others. Progress has been slower perpendicularly, but incomparably greater because lateral. He has not taught his contemporaries to climb mountains, but he has persuaded the villagers to move up a few feet higher; added to this, he has made secure his progress. A few months after the death of the promoter of this model town, a court decision made it obligatory upon the company to divest itself of the management of the town as involving a function beyond its corporate powers. The parks, flowers, and fountains of this far-famed industrial centre were dismantled, with scarcely a protest from the inhabitants themselves.

===Chinese===
坚持要认可、与民众同行的人，便不得不既求教于「可行的正当」（feasible right），也求教于绝对的正当。他常常只能求得林肯先生（Mr. Lincoln）所说的「可能中的最好」，继而生出与自己的最高信念相妥协的那种令人作呕之感。他不得不与他所引领的人们一道，朝着他自己和他们都不甚看得清的目标前进——直到抵达时才看清。他不得不去发现人们真正想要什么，然后「提供渠道，使他们生活中日益成长的道德力量得以流淌其中」。然而他所达到的，并非他个人奋斗的结果——不像孤身的登山者之超越谷中众人那样——而是被许多人的情感与抱负支撑着、托举着。就垂直的方向而言，进展慢了些；但因为它是横向的，便无可比拟地更为宏大。他没有教会他的同代人登山，却说服了村民们向上挪高了几英尺；不但如此，他还使自己的进展稳当可靠。在这座模范城镇的发起人死后几个月，一纸法院判决责令该公司放弃对城镇的管理权，理由是它牵涉一项超出公司权限的职能。这座遐迩闻名的产业中心，其公园、花木与喷泉被一一拆除，居民们自己几乎未出一声抗议。

===Original===
The man who disassociates his ambition, however disinterested, from the cooperation of his fellows, always takes this risk of ultimate failure. He does not take advantage of the great conserver and guarantee of his own permanent success which associated efforts afford. Genuine experiments toward higher social conditions must have a more democratic faith and practice than those which underlie private venture. Public parks and improvements, intended for the common use, are after all only safe in the hands of the public itself; and associated effort toward social progress, although much more awkward and stumbling than that same effort managed by a capable individual, does yet enlist deeper forces and evoke higher social capacities.

===Chinese===
一个人无论多么无私，一旦使自己的抱负脱离同伴的合作，便总要冒这最终失败的风险。他不曾借重联合努力所提供的那伟大的保全之力与担保——那本是他持久成功的担保。朝着更高的社会条件迈进的真正实验，必须抱持比私人事业所依据的更民主的信念与实践。为公共使用而设的公园与公共改良，归根结底只有握在公众自己手里才安全；朝着社会进步的联合努力，尽管比能干的个人管理下的同样努力笨拙得多、踉跄得多，却能征召更深层的力量，唤起更高的社会才能。

===Original===
The successful business man who is also the philanthropist is in more than the usual danger of getting widely separated from his employees. The men already have the American veneration for wealth and successful business capacity, and, added to this, they are dazzled by his good works. The workmen have the same kindly impulses as he, but while they organize their charity into mutual benefit associations and distribute their money in small amounts in relief for the widows and insurance for the injured, the employer may build model towns, erect college buildings, which are tangible and enduring, and thereby display his goodness in concentrated form.

===Chinese===
既是成功商人又是慈善家的人，比寻常更有与其雇工远远隔开的危险。工人们本就怀着美国式的对财富与成功商业才干的崇敬，加上又被他的善行弄得目眩。工人们怀着与他同样的仁慈冲动，可当他们把慈善组织成互济会（mutual benefit association）、把小笔小笔的钱散作寡妇的救济与伤者的保险时，雇主却可以建造模范城镇、竖起学院大楼——它们有形而经久——从而以凝聚的形式展示他的善。

===Original===
By the very exigencies of business demands, the employer is too often cut off from the social ethics developing in regard to our larger social relationships, and from the great moral life springing from our common experiences. This is sure to happen when he is good “to” people rather than “with” them, when he allows himself to decide what is best for them instead of consulting them. He thus misses the rectifying influence of that fellowship which is so big that it leaves no room for sensitiveness or gratitude. Without this fellowship we may never know how great the divergence between ourselves and others may become, nor how cruel the misunderstandings.

===Chinese===
恰恰是生意要求的紧迫，使雇主太容易被隔绝在我们更宏大的社会关系中日渐发展的社会伦理之外，隔绝在从我们共同经验里生长出来的伟大道德生活之外。当他是「对」人们行善、而不是「与」人们一起行善时，当他任由自己替人们决定什么最好、而不与人们相商时，这种隔绝便注定发生。他因此错失了那种同伴情谊的矫正之力——那情谊如此博大，以致不给敏感或感恩留下任何余地。没有这种情谊，我们也许永远无从知道，自己与他人之间的分歧会变得多大，误会会有多残忍。

===Original===
During a recent strike of the employees of a large factory in Ohio, the president of the company expressed himself as bitterly disappointed by the results of his many kindnesses, and evidently considered the employees utterly unappreciative. His state of mind was the result of the fallacy of ministering to social needs from an individual impulse and expecting a socialized return of gratitude and loyalty. If the lunchroom was necessary, it was a necessity in order that the employees might have better food, and, when they had received the better food, the legitimate aim of the lunchroom was met. If baths were desirable, and the fifteen minutes of calisthenic exercise given the women in the middle of each half day brought a needed rest and change to their muscles, then the increased cleanliness and the increased bodily comfort of so many people should of themselves have justified the experiment.

===Chinese===
俄亥俄（Ohio）一家大工厂的雇工不久前罢工，其间公司总裁表示，自己诸多厚意换来的结果令他痛心失望，显然认为雇工们全然不知感念。他的这种心态，出自一种谬误：以个人的冲动去满足社会的需要，却指望得到社会化的感恩与忠诚的回报。午餐室若是必需的，那必需性便在于让雇工吃上更好的饭食；当他们吃上了更好的饭食，午餐室正当的目的便已达成。浴室若是可取的，每个半天中间给女工们的十五分钟体操锻炼若确给她们的肌肉带来必需的休息与调剂，那么这许许多多人清洁的增进与身体舒适的增进，本身便足以证明这项实验的正当。

===Original===
To demand, as a further result, that there should be no strikes in the factory, no revolt against the will of the employer because the employees were filled with loyalty as the result of the kindness, was of course to take the experiment from an individual basis to a social one.

===Chinese===
若还进一步要求：工厂里从此不得有罢工，雇工们不得违逆雇主的意志，因为他们已因这份厚意而满怀忠诚——那当然便是把这项实验从个人的基础搬到社会的基础上去了。

===Original===
Large mining companies and manufacturing concerns are constantly appealing to their stockholders for funds, or for permission to take a percentage of the profits, in order that the money may be used for educational and social schemes designed for the benefit of the employees. The promoters of these schemes use as an argument and as an appeal, that better relations will be thus established, that strikes will be prevented, and that in the end the money returned to the stockholders will be increased. However praiseworthy this appeal may be in motive, it involves a distinct confusion of issues, and in theory deserves the failure it so often meets with in practice. In the clash which follows a strike, the employees are accused of an ingratitude, when there was no legitimate reason to expect gratitude; and useless bitterness, which has really a factitious basis, may be developed on both sides.

===Chinese===
大型矿业公司与制造企业不断向股东呼吁资金，或请求允许从利润中提取一个百分比，好把这笔钱用于为雇工谋福利的教育与社交方案。这些方案的倡导者用作论据、用作呼吁的是：如此便能建立更好的关系，罢工得以防止，最终返还股东的钱还会增多。这呼吁用意无论多么可嘉，其中都牵涉一种分明的是非混淆，在理论上便理应得到它在实践中屡屡遭遇的失败。罢工随之而来的冲突中，雇工被指控忘恩负义，而其实本没有正当的理由去期待感恩；于是双方都可能生发出一种实在人为的、无谓的怨毒。

===Original===
Indeed, unless the relation becomes a democratic one, the chances of misunderstanding are increased, when to the relation of employer and employees is added the relation of benefactor to beneficiaries, in so far as there is still another opportunity for acting upon the individual code of ethics.

===Chinese===
的确，除非这关系变成民主的关系，否则，当雇主与雇工的关系之上又叠加了施惠者与受惠者的关系时，误会的机会只会增加，因为那不过是又多了一个循个人伦理法典行事的机会。

===Original===
There is no doubt that these efforts are to be commended, not only from the standpoint of their social value but because they have a marked industrial significance. Failing, as they do, however, to touch the question of wages and hours, which are almost invariably the points of trades-union effort, the employers confuse the mind of the public when they urge the amelioration of conditions and the kindly relation existing between them and their men as a reason for the discontinuance of strikes and other trades-union tactics. The men have individually accepted the kindness of the employers as it was individually offered, but quite as the latter urges his inability to increase wages unless he has the cooperation of his competitors, so the men state that they are bound to the trades-union struggle for an increase in wages because it can only be undertaken by combinations of labor.

===Chinese===
毫无疑问，这些努力值得称许，不仅从其社会价值的立场看是如此，而且因为它们具有显著的产业意义。然而，由于这些努力并未触及工资与工时的问题——那几乎一成不变地是工会努力的着力点——雇主们把境况的改良与劳资之间现存的善意关系，当作罢工及其他工会策略应当停止的理由来申说时，便把公众的头脑搅乱了。工人们已经以个人的方式，接受了雇主以个人方式给出的善意；可正像雇主申言，没有竞争者的合作他便无法增加工资一样，工人们也说，他们注定要为增加工资而进行工会的斗争，因为那只有靠劳工的联合才能着手。

===Original===
Even the much more democratic effort to divide a proportion of the profits at the end of the year among the employees, upon the basis of their wages and efficiency, is also exposed to a weakness, from the fact that the employing side has the power of determining to whom the benefit shall accrue.

===Chinese===
就连年终按工资与效率在雇工中间分配一份利润这种民主得多的努力，也有它的软处：雇主一方握有决定利益归于谁的权力。

===Original===
Both individual acts of self-defence on the part of the wage earner and individual acts of benevolence on the part of the employer are most useful as they establish standards to which the average worker and employer may in time be legally compelled to conform. Progress must always come through the individual who varies from the type and has sufficient energy to express this variation. He first holds a higher conception than that held by the mass of his fellows of what is righteous under given conditions, and expresses this conviction in conduct, in many instances formulating a certain scruple which the others share, but have not yet defined even to themselves. Progress, however, is not secure until the mass has conformed to this new righteousness. This is equally true in regard to any advance made in the standard of living on the part of the trades-unionists or in the improved conditions of industry on the part of reforming employers. The mistake lies, not in overpraising the advance thus inaugurated by individual initiative, but in regarding the achievement as complete in a social sense when it is still in the realm of individual action.

===Chinese===
挣工资者的个人自卫之举与雇主的个人仁善之举，都极有用处，因为它们确立了标准，寻常的工人与雇主终有一日可在法律上被迫遵从。进步总要通过这样的个人而来：他偏离常型，又有足够的精力把这一变异表达出来。他率先持有一种比同侪大众更高的观念，认为在给定条件下何为正当，并把这一信念表达于行为之中；在许多情形下，他还把旁人也怀有、却连对自己都尚未界定的某种顾忌明确地表述出来。然而，要等大众都遵从了这新的正当，进步才算稳固。这一点，对工会会员一方在生活水准上的任何提高，与改革中的雇主一方在产业条件上的任何改善，同样成立。错误不在于过高赞誉由个人主动开启的这种进步，而在于当成就仍停留在个人行动的领域时，便认为它在社会的意义上已经完成。

===Original===
No sane manufacturer regards his factory as the centre of the industrial system. He knows very well that the cost of material, wages, and selling prices are determined by industrial conditions completely beyond his control. Yet the same man may quite calmly regard himself and his own private principles as merely self-regarding, and expect results from casual philanthropy which can only be accomplished through those common rules of life and labor established by the community for the common good.

===Chinese===
没有一个头脑健全的制造业主把自己的工厂看作产业体系的中心。他清楚得很，原料的成本、工资与售价，都由完全超出他控制的产业状况决定。然而同一个人，却可以相当平静地把自己和自己私人的原则看作只关一己之事，指望从随手施与的慈善里得到那样的结果——那结果，唯有通过共同体为公共善而确立的那些生活与劳动的共同规则才能达成。

===Original===
Outside of and surrounding these smaller and most significant efforts are the larger and irresistible movements operating toward combination. This movement must tend to decide upon social matters from the social standpoint. Until then it is difficult to keep our minds free from a confusion of issues. Such a confusion occurs when the gift of a large sum to the community for a public and philanthropic purpose, throws a certain glamour over all the earlier acts of a man, and makes it difficult for the community to see possible wrongs committed against it, in the accumulation of wealth so beneficently used. It is possible also that the resolve to be thus generous unconsciously influences the man himself in his methods of accumulation. He keeps to a certain individual rectitude, meaning to make an individual restitution by the old paths of generosity and kindness, whereas if he had in view social restitution on the newer lines of justice and opportunity, he would throughout his course doubtless be watchful of his industrial relationships and his social virtues.

===Chinese===
在这些较小却极有分量的努力之外并环绕着它们的，是朝着联合运作的更大而不可抗拒的运动。这一运动势必趋于从社会的立场决断社会的事务。在那之前，我们的头脑很难免于是非混淆。比如，一个人为公共的与慈善的目的向共同体捐出一大笔钱，这笔馈赠便给他先前的全部行事罩上一重光彩，使共同体难以看清：在那笔用得如此仁善的财富的积累过程中，可能有过加诸它自身的伤害——混淆便这样发生了。同样可能的是，决意如此慷慨，也在不知不觉中影响了这人自己的积累方法。他固守着某种个人的正直，打算沿慷慨与仁慈的旧径作出个人的补偿；倘若他心中所想的是沿着正义与机会的新路作出的社会补偿，那么在整个过程中，他无疑会时时留心自己的产业关系与社会德行。

===Original===
The danger of professionally attaining to the power of the righteous man, of yielding to the ambition “for doing good” on a large scale, compared to which the ambition for politics, learning, or wealth, are vulgar and commonplace, ramifies through our modern life; and those most easily beset by this temptation are precisely the men best situated to experiment on the larger social lines, because they so easily dramatize their acts and lead public opinion. Very often, too, they have in their hands the preservation and advancement of large vested interests, and often see clearly and truly that they are better able to administer the affairs of the community than the community itself: sometimes they see that if they do not administer them sharply and quickly, as only an individual can, certain interests of theirs dependent upon the community will go to ruin.

===Chinese===
把修得义人权柄当作专业、向大规模「行善」的野心屈服——与这野心相比，对政治、学问或财富的野心都显得庸俗而平淡——这种危险在现代生活中枝节丛生；而最容易受这诱惑围困的，恰恰是那些最有条件在更宏大的社会路线上做实验的人，因为他们太容易把自己的行为戏剧化、引领公众舆论。他们又往往手握庞大既得利益的保全与增进，而且常常看得清楚而真切：管理共同体的事务，他们比共同体自己更在行。有时他们还看到：倘若不像一个个人所能的那样干脆迅速地加以管理，他们那些仰赖共同体的利益便将败坏。

===Original===
The model employer first considered, provided a large sum in his will with which to build and equip a polytechnic school, which will doubtless be of great public value. This again shows the advantage of individual management, in the spending as well as in the accumulating of wealth, but this school will attain its highest good, in so far as it incites the ambition to provide other schools from public funds. The town of Zurich possesses a magnificent polytechnic institute, secured by the vote of the entire people and supported from public taxes. Every man who voted for it is interested that his child should enjoy its benefits, and, of course, the voluntary attendance must be larger than in a school accepted as a gift to the community.

===Chinese===
前面首先讨论的那位模范雇主，在遗嘱中拨出一大笔钱，用于建造并装备一所理工学校（polytechnic school），它无疑将具有巨大的公共价值。这再次显示了个人管理的优势，无论在财富的积累上还是在花费上；但这座学校唯有激起人们从公共资金中创办其他学校的雄心，才算达到它最高的善。苏黎世（Zurich）这座城拥有一座宏伟的理工学院，它由全体人民的投票赢得，由公共税收维持。每一个投了赞成票的人，都关切着自己的孩子能享用它的好处；而且，自愿入学的人数当然必定多于一所被当作礼物接受下来的学校。

===Original===
In the educational efforts of model employers, as in other attempts toward social amelioration, one man with the best of intentions is trying to do what the entire body of employees should have undertaken to do for themselves. The result of his efforts will only attain its highest value as it serves as an incentive to procure other results by the community as well as for the community.

===Chinese===
在模范雇主们的教育努力中，正如在其他社会改良的尝试中一样，一个人怀着最好的用意，去做全体雇工本应为自己着手去做的事。他的努力的结果，唯有当它成为一种激励，促使共同体为共同体自己去取得其他成果时，才达到它的最高价值。

===Original===
There are doubtless many things which the public would never demand unless they were first supplied by individual initiative, both because the public lacks the imagination, and also the power of formulating their wants. Thus philanthropic effort supplies kindergartens, until they become so established in the popular affections that they are incorporated in the public school system. Churches and missions establish reading rooms, until at last the public library system dots the city with branch reading rooms and libraries. For this willingness to take risks for the sake of an ideal, for those experiments which must be undertaken with vigor and boldness in order to secure didactic value in failure as well as in success, society must depend upon the individual possessed with money, and also distinguished by earnest and unselfish purpose. Such experiments enable the nation to use the Referendum method in its public affairs. Each social experiment is thus tested by a few people, given wide publicity, that it may be observed and discussed by the bulk of the citizens before the public prudently makes up its mind whether or not it is wise to incorporate it into the functions of government. If the decision is in its favor and it is so incorporated, it can then be carried on with confidence and enthusiasm.

===Chinese===
毫无疑问，有许多东西，公众绝不会主动要求，除非它们先由个人的主动性供给出来——既因为公众缺乏想象力，也缺乏把愿望表述成型的能力。于是，慈善事业办起幼儿园，直到幼儿园在民众的感情中扎根如此之深，以致被并入公共学校制度。教会与布道团设立阅览室，直到最后，公共图书馆制度用遍布全城的分馆阅览室与图书馆点满了这座城市。至于为理想而甘冒风险的这份意愿，至于那些必须以魄力与大胆从事、以便在失败中亦如在成功中一样取得教训价值的实验，社会必须仰仗这样的人：既有钱财，又以诚挚无私的志向见称。这样的实验使国家得以在公共事务上使用公民投票（Referendum）的方法。每一项社会实验就这样先由少数人试行，再广而告之，让大多数公民观察、讨论，然后公众才审慎地拿定主意：把它并入政府的职能是否明智。倘若决议赞成、它果真被并入，它便可以带着信心与热忱继续办下去了。

===Original===
But experience has shown that we can only depend upon successful men for a certain type of experiment in the line of industrial amelioration and social advancement. The list of those who found churches, educational institutions, libraries, and art galleries, is very long, as is again the list of those contributing to model dwellings, recreation halls, and athletic fields. At the present moment factory employers are doing much to promote “industrial betterment” in the way of sanitary surroundings, opportunities for bathing, lunch rooms provided with cheap and wholesome food, club rooms, and guild halls. But there is a line of social experiment involving social righteousness in its most advanced form, in which the number of employers and the “favored class” are so few that it is plain society cannot count upon them for continuous and valuable help. This lack is in the line of factory legislation and that sort of social advance implied in shorter hours and the regulation of wages; in short, all that organization and activity that is involved in such a maintenance and increase of wages as would prevent the lowering of the standard of life.

===Chinese===
然而经验已经表明，在产业改良与社会进步的路线上，我们只能指望成功人士做某一类型的实验。创办教会、教育机构、图书馆与美术馆的人，名单很长；捐建模范住宅、娱乐会堂与运动场的人，名单同样不短。此时此刻，工厂雇主们正大力推动「产业改善」（industrial betterment）：卫生的环境、洗浴的机会、供应廉价而卫生饭食的午餐室、俱乐部室与行会会堂。但还有一路社会实验，涉及最先进形式的社会正义，在那一路里，雇主与「受优待的阶级」（favored class）人数如此之少，以致社会显然无法指望他们提供持续而宝贵的帮助。这一路所缺的，是工厂立法（factory legislation），是由缩短工时与规制工资所蕴含的那种社会进步；一言以蔽之，是那样一种为维持并增加工资、以防生活水准下降所牵涉的全部组织与活动。

===Original===
A large body of people feel keenly that the present industrial system is in a state of profound disorder, and that there is no guarantee that the pursuit of individual ethics will ever right it. They claim that relief can only come through deliberate corporate effort inspired by social ideas and guided by the study of economic laws, and that the present industrial system thwarts our ethical demands, not only for social righteousness but for social order. Because they believe that each advance in ethics must be made fast by a corresponding advance in politics and legal enactment, they insist upon the right of state regulation and control. While many people representing all classes in a community would assent to this as to a general proposition, and would even admit it as a certain moral obligation, legislative enactments designed to control industrial conditions have largely been secured through the efforts of a few citizens, mostly those who constantly see the harsh conditions of labor and who are incited to activity by their sympathies as well as their convictions.

===Chinese===
有一大群人痛切地感到：现存的产业制度处于深刻的失序之中，而追逐个人伦理并无任何保证能使它复正。他们声称，救济只能来自由社会观念激发、由对经济法则的研究引导的深思熟虑的社团努力；现存的产业制度挫败着我们的伦理要求——不仅对社会正义的要求，也对社会秩序的要求。因为他们相信，伦理上的每一步前进，都必须由政治与法律制定上相应的前进加以锁定，所以他们坚持国家管制与监督的权利。尽管一个共同体中代表各阶级的许许多多人会把它当作一个一般命题表示同意，甚至承认它是一项道德义务，但旨在控制产业条件的立法，大抵是靠少数公民的努力才争得的——他们多半不断目睹着劳动的严酷境况，被自己的同情与信念一同激起来行动。

===Original===
This may be illustrated by the series of legal enactments regulating the occupations in which children may be allowed to work, also the laws in regard to the hours of labor permitted in those occupations, and the minimum age below which children may not be employed. The first child labor laws were enacted in England through the efforts of those members of parliament whose hearts were wrung by the condition of the little parish apprentices bound out to the early textile manufacturers of the north; and through the long years required to build up the code of child labor legislation which England now possesses, knowledge of the conditions has always preceded effective legislation. The efforts of that small number in every community who believe in legislative control have always been reinforced by the efforts of trades-unionists rather than by the efforts of employers. Partly because the employment of workingmen in the factories brings them in contact with the children who tend to lower wages and demoralize their trades, and partly because workingmen have no money nor time to spend in alleviating philanthropy, and must perforce seize upon agitation and legal enactment as the only channel of redress which is open to them.

===Chinese===
这一点，可以用一系列法律条文来说明：它们规定儿童可以被允许从事哪些职业，规定那些职业里许可的劳动时数，规定不得雇用儿童的最低年龄。最早的童工法是在英国制定的，靠的是那些议会议员的努力——那些被契送给北方早期纺织制造商做学徒的小小教区学徒，其境况绞着他们的心；而在建成英国今日拥有的这套童工立法法典所需的漫长岁月里，对境况的了解总是先于有效的立法。每个共同体里那少数相信立法管制的人，他们的努力所得到的增援，一直来自工会会员，而不是来自雇主。一部分是因为工厂里做工的人直接接触到那些往往压低工资、败坏他们行当的童工，一部分是因为工人既没有钱、也没有时间花在缓解式的慈善上，便只得抓住鼓动与立法这一条向他们敞开的救济渠道。

===Original===
We may illustrate by imagining a row of people seated in a moving streetcar, into which darts a boy of eight, calling out the details of the last murder, in the hope of selling an evening newspaper. A comfortable looking man buys a paper from him with no sense of moral shock; he may even be a trifle complacent that he has helped along the little fellow, who is making his way in the world. The philanthropic lady sitting next to him may perhaps reflect that it is a pity that such a bright boy is not in school. She may make up her mind in a moment of compunction to redouble her efforts for various newsboys’ schools and homes, that this poor child may have better teaching, and perhaps a chance at manual training. She probably is convinced that he alone, by his unaided efforts, is supporting a widowed mother, and her heart is moved to do all she can for him. Next to her sits a workingman trained in trades-union methods. He knows that the boy’s natural development is arrested, and that the abnormal activity of his body and mind uses up the force which should go into growth; moreover, that this premature use of his powers has but a momentary and specious value. He is forced to these conclusions because he has seen many a man, entering the factory at eighteen and twenty, so worn out by premature work that he was “laid on the shelf” within ten or fifteen years. He knows very well that he can do nothing in the way of ameliorating the lot of this particular boy; that his only possible chance is to agitate for proper child-labor laws; to regulate, and if possible prohibit, street-vending by children, in order that the child of the poorest may have his school time secured to him, and may have at least his short chance for growth.

===Chinese===
我们可以设想这样一幅图景：一节行进中的有轨电车里坐着一排人，一个八岁的男孩蹿上车来，喊叫着最新一桩凶杀案的种种详情，指望卖出一份晚报。一位模样安逸的先生从他手里买了份报，毫无道德上的震动；他甚至可能有点自得，觉得自己帮了这小家伙一把——这孩子正在世上自谋出路呢。坐在他旁边的那位慈善的太太，或许会想到：这么伶俐的男孩竟不在学校，真可惜。她可能在心头一阵刺痛之下打定主意，要为各式各样的报童学校与收容所加倍出力，好让这可怜的孩子得到更好的教导，或许还有学一点手工的机会。她多半还确信，这孩子全凭一己之力养活着寡母，于是心里一动，要为他竭尽所能。在太太旁边坐着一个受过工会方法训练的工人。他知道，这孩子的自然发育已被遏止，身心那反常的活跃，耗尽了本该流入生长的力量；而且，这种对体力的过早动用，只有一时的、虚有其表的价值。他不得不得出这些结论，因为他见过太多这样的人：十八岁、二十岁进厂，被过早的劳作磨损得厉害，不出十年十五年便被「搁上了架子」。他清楚得很，要改善这个具体男孩的命运，他什么也做不了；他唯一可能的机会，是为像样的童工法而鼓动，去规制、并尽可能禁止儿童的街头叫卖，好让最穷的人家的孩子也保得住他的上学时光，至少保得住他那一段短暂的生长机会。

===Original===
These three people, sitting in the streetcar, are all honest and upright, and recognize a certain duty toward the forlorn children of the community. The self-made man is encouraging one boy’s own efforts; the philanthropic lady is helping on a few boys; the workingman alone is obliged to include all the boys of his class. Workingmen, because of their feebleness in all but numbers, have been forced to appeal to the state, in order to secure protection for themselves and for their children. They cannot all rise out of their class, as the occasionally successful man has done; some of them must be left to do the work in the factories and mines, and they have no money to spend in philanthropy.

===Chinese===
电车里的这三个人都诚实正直，也都承认对社区中孤苦的孩子负有一定的义务。白手起家的先生鼓励的是一个男孩自己的努力；慈善的太太帮衬的是几个男孩；唯独那工人，不得不把他那个阶级的所有男孩都包括进来。劳动的人们，因为除了人数之外处处薄弱，被迫向国家呼吁，为自己、为自己的孩子求得保护。他们不可能人人都像偶尔成功的人那样脱离本阶级；他们中的一些人必须留在工厂和矿井里做工，而且他们没有钱花在慈善上。

===Original===
Both public agitation and a social appeal to the conscience of the community is necessary in order to secure help from the state, and, curiously enough, child-labor laws once enacted and enforced are a matter of great pride, and even come to be regarded as a register of the community’s humanity and enlightenment. If the method of public agitation could find quiet and orderly expression in legislative enactment, and if labor measures could be submitted to the examination and judgment of the whole without a sense of division or of warfare, we should have the ideal development of the democratic state.

===Chinese===
要赢得国家的帮助，公开的鼓动与向共同体良心的社会呼吁，两者都不可少；而说来奇怪，童工法一经制定并执行，便成了一大骄傲，甚至渐渐被看作共同体仁爱与开明程度的记录。倘若公开鼓动的方法能够在立法中得到安静而有序的表达，倘若劳工措施能够被交付全体来审查与判断、而不带分裂或交战之感，我们便有了民主国家的理想发展。

===Original===
But we judge labor organizations as we do other living institutions, not by their declaration of principles, which we seldom read, but by their blundering efforts to apply their principles to actual conditions, and by the oft-time failure of their representatives, when the individual finds himself too weak to become the organ of corporate action.

===Chinese===
可是我们评判劳工组织，就像评判其他活的制度一样，不是凭它们的原则宣言——那我们很少去读——而是凭它们把原则应用于实际境况时的种种笨拙努力，凭它们代表们屡见的失败：那时个人发现自己太弱，当不成社团行动的器官。

===Original===
The very blunders and lack of organization too often characterizing a union, in marked contrast to the orderly management of a factory, often confuse us as to the real issues involved, and we find it hard to trust uncouth and unruly manifestations of social effort. The situation is made even more complicated by the fact that those who are formulating a code of associated action so often break through the established code of law and order. As society has a right to demand of the reforming individual that he be sternly held to his personal and domestic claims, so it has a right to insist that labor organizations shall keep to the hardly won standards of public law and order; and the community performs but its plain duty when it registers its protest every time law and order are subverted, even in the interest of the so-called social effort. Yet in moments of industrial stress and strain the community is confronted by a moral perplexity which may arise from the mere fact that the good of yesterday is opposed to the good of today, and that which may appear as a choice between virtue and vice is really but a choice between virtue and virtue. In the disorder and confusion sometimes incident to growth and progress, the community may be unable to see anything but the unlovely struggle itself.

===Chinese===
工会常常带着种种失误与组织的缺乏，与工厂井然的管理恰成对照；这往往把我们搅糊涂，看不清真正的问题所在，我们也便难以信任社会努力那粗野不驯的表现。局面又因这样一桩事实而更加复杂：正在制订联合行动法典的那些人，又常常闯破既定的法律与秩序的法典。正如社会有权要求改革中的个人恪守加于他的个人与家庭义务，社会也有权坚持劳工组织恪守来之不易的公共法律与秩序的标准；每当法律与秩序被颠覆——哪怕是为了所谓社会努力的利益——共同体都提出抗议，而那不过是履行它明白的义务。然而，在产业紧张与拉扯的时刻，共同体面对的是一种道德的惶惑，它可能仅仅出自这样一桩事实：昨日的善与今日的善相抵牾；那看似德性与恶行之间的选择，其实只是德性与德性之间的选择。在有时伴随生长与进步而来的失序与混乱之中，共同体也许除了那场不体面的挣扎本身，什么也看不见。

===Original===
The writer recalls a conversation between two workingmen who were leaving a lecture on “Organic Evolution.” The first was much puzzled, and anxiously inquired of the second “if evolution could mean that one animal turned into another.” The challenged workman stopped in the rear of the hall, put his foot upon a chair, and expounded what he thought evolution did mean; and this, so nearly as the conversation can be recalled, is what he said: “You see a lot of fishes are living in a stream, which overflows in the spring and strands some of them upon the bank. The weak ones die up there, but others make a big effort to get back into the water. They dig their fins into the sand, breathe as much air as they can with their gills, and have a terrible time. But after a while their fins turn into legs and their gills into lungs, and they have become frogs. Of course they are further along than the sleek, comfortable fishes who sail up and down the stream waving their tails and despising the poor damaged things thrashing around on the bank. He﻿—the lecturer﻿—did not say anything about men, but it is easy enough to think of us poor devils on the dry bank, struggling without enough to live on, while the comfortable fellows sail along in the water with all they want and despise us because we thrash about.” His listener did not reply, and was evidently dissatisfied both with the explanation and the application. Doubtless the illustration was bungling in more than its setting forth, but the story is suggestive.

===Chinese===
作者记得两个工人的一场谈话，他们刚听完一场题为「有机进化」（Organic Evolution）的讲座出来。第一个满脸困惑，急切地问第二个：「进化是不是可能意味着一种动物变成了另一种动物。」被问的工人就在会场后面停住脚，一只脚踏上椅子，讲起他心目中进化到底是什么意思；就谈话所能追忆的，他说的大致是这样：「你瞧，一群鱼住在一条河里，春天河水泛滥，把一些鱼搁浅在岸上。弱的就在上面死了，别的则拼命想回到水里去。它们把鳍插进沙里，用鳃尽量多吸点空气，日子苦不堪言。可过了一阵，它们的鳍变成了腿，鳃变成了肺，它们成了青蛙。它们当然比那些在河里游上游下、摆着尾巴、看不起岸上那些挣扎的可怜东西的光滑适意的鱼更靠前。他——讲的人——没有讲到人，可把我们这些旱岸上的可怜鬼想想就很容易了：我们挣扎着，连糊口都不够，而那些舒舒服服的家伙在水里什么都有地漂着，还因为我们扑腾而看不起我们。」听的人没有回答，显然对这解释和这应用都不满意。无疑，这个比方的笨拙不止在于它的陈述；但这故事发人深省。

===Original===
At times of social disturbance the law-abiding citizen is naturally so anxious for peace and order, his sympathies are so justly and inevitably on the side making for the restoration of law, that it is difficult for him to see the situation fairly. He becomes insensible to the unselfish impulse which may prompt a sympathetic strike in behalf of the workers in a nonunion shop, because he allows his mind to dwell exclusively on the disorder which has become associated with the strike. He is completely sidetracked by the ugly phases of a great moral movement. It is always a temptation to assume that the side which has respectability, authority, and superior intelligence, has therefore righteousness as well, especially when the same side presents concrete results of individual effort as over against the less tangible results of associated effort.

===Chinese===
社会骚动的时刻，守法的公民自然渴求和平与秩序，他的同情又如此正当而必然地站在恢复法律的一边，以致他很难公允地看清局面。他变得对那可能促使人们声援非工会工厂工人而发动同情罢工的无私冲动毫无感觉，因为他任凭自己的心思单单盘桓在已与罢工连在一起的骚乱上。他被一场伟大道德运动的丑陋诸相彻底带进了岔道。人们总禁不住以为，拥有体面、权威与优越智力的那一方，因此也就拥有了正义——尤其当这一方摆出个人努力的具体成果、与联合努力那不那么可触的成果相对时，更是如此。

===Original===
It is as yet most difficult for us to free ourselves from the individualistic point of view sufficiently to group events in their social relations and to judge fairly those who are endeavoring to produce a social result through all the difficulties of associated action. The philanthropist still finds his path much easier than do those who are attempting a social morality. In the first place, the public, anxious to praise what it recognizes as an undoubted moral effort often attended with real personal sacrifice, joyfully seizes upon this manifestation and overpraises it, recognizing the philanthropist as an old friend in the paths of righteousness, whereas the others are strangers and possibly to be distrusted as aliens. It is easy to confuse the response to an abnormal number of individual claims with the response to the social claim. An exaggerated personal morality is often mistaken for a social morality, and until it attempts to minister to a social situation its total inadequacy is not discovered. To attempt to attain a social morality without a basis of democratic experience results in the loss of the only possible corrective and guide, and ends in an exaggerated individual morality but not in social morality at all. We see this from time to time in the careworn and overworked philanthropist, who has taxed his individual will beyond the normal limits and has lost his clue to the situation among a bewildering number of cases. A man who takes the betterment of humanity for his aim and end must also take the daily experiences of humanity for the constant correction of his process. He must not only test and guide his achievement by human experience, but he must succeed or fail in proportion as he has incorporated that experience with his own. Otherwise his own achievements become his stumbling-block, and he comes to believe in his own goodness as something outside of himself. He makes an exception of himself, and thinks that he is different from the rank and file of his fellows. He forgets that it is necessary to know of the lives of our contemporaries, not only in order to believe in their integrity, which is after all but the first beginnings of social morality, but in order to attain to any mental or moral integrity for ourselves or any such hope for society.

===Chinese===
时至今日，我们仍极难把自己从个人主义的观点中解放出来，难到足以把事件按其社会关系归类，公允地评判那些正穿越联合行动的种种困难、努力产出社会结果的人。慈善家的路，至今仍比那些尝试社会道德的人好走得多。首先，公众急切地要赞扬它认出的那种无可置疑的道德努力——这种努力往往伴随着真正的个人牺牲——于是欣然抓住这一表现而赞誉过了头，把慈善家认作正义之途上的老朋友，而把另一些人当作生人，还可能当作异己而心存疑忌。对数量反常的种种个人诉求的回应，很容易与对社会性诉求（the social claim）的回应混为一谈。一种被夸张的个人道德（personal morality）常被误认作社会道德，而非等到它试图服务于一个社会局面时，它的全然不足是不会被发现的。没有民主经验作基础而试图求得社会道德，结果是失去唯一可能的矫正与指引，终究得到一种夸张的个人道德，而根本不是社会道德。这一点，我们不时在忧劳过度的慈善家身上看到：他把个人的意志驱策到正常限度之外，在一大堆令人目眩的个案中间丢失了把握局面的线索。以人类的改善为目标与归宿的人，还必须以人类的日常经验，为他方法的持续矫正。他不仅必须以人的经验来检验和引导他的成就，而且他能否成功，全系于他把那经验与自己的经验融会到什么程度。否则，他自己的成就便成了他的绊脚石，他渐渐相信自己的善是某种外在于他的东西。他把自己当作例外，认为自己与同侪的芸芸众生不同。他忘记了：了解同时代人的生活是必要的——不仅是为了相信他们的正直（那毕竟只是社会道德的最初开端），更是为了我们自己获得任何心智上或道德上的正直，或对社会怀有任何这样的希望。

## VI Educational Methods / 教育方法

===Original===
As democracy modifies our conception of life, it constantly raises the value and function of each member of the community, however humble he may be. We have come to believe that the most “brutish man” has a value in our common life, a function to perform which can be fulfilled by no one else. We are gradually requiring of the educator that he shall free the powers of each man and connect him with the rest of life. We ask this not merely because it is the man’s right to be thus connected, but because we have become convinced that the social order cannot afford to get along without his special contribution. Just as we have come to resent all hindrances which keep us from untrammelled comradeship with our fellows, and as we throw down unnatural divisions, not in the spirit of the eighteenth-century reformers, but in the spirit of those to whom social equality has become a necessity for further social development, so we are impatient to use the dynamic power residing in the mass of men, and demand that the educator free that power. We believe that man’s moral idealism is the constructive force of progress, as it has always been; but because every human being is a creative agent and a possible generator of fine enthusiasm, we are sceptical of the moral idealism of the few and demand the education of the many, that there may be greater freedom, strength, and subtlety of intercourse and hence an increase of dynamic power. We are not content to include all men in our hopes, but have become conscious that all men are hoping and are part of the same movement of which we are a part.

===Chinese===
民主既然修正着我们的生命观念，便不断抬高共同体每一成员——无论他多么卑微——的价值与功用。我们已经渐渐相信，连「最粗野的人」在共同生活中也有其价值，也有一项非他莫属的职能要履行。我们正逐步要求教育者去解放每个人的种种能力，把他与其余的生活联结起来。我们提出这一要求，不仅因为这样被联结是这个人的权利，更因为我们已经确信：缺少了他的特殊贡献，社会秩序是维持不下去的。正如我们已开始愤懑于一切阻碍我们与同胞不受羁绊地结为伙伴的东西，正如我们正在推倒种种不自然的分隔——不是本着十八世纪改革者的精神，而是本着那样一些人的精神，对他们来说，社会平等已成为进一步的社会发展之必需——我们也急不可待地要动用蕴藏在民众之中的那股动力，并要求教育者把这力量释放出来。我们相信，人的道德理想主义一如既往仍是进步的建设性力量；但正因为每一个人都是创造性的行动者，都可能生出美好的热情，我们对少数人的道德理想主义心存怀疑，而要求对多数人施行教育，好使交往获得更大的自由、力量与精微，从而使动力得以增长。我们已不满足于把所有的人纳入我们的希望之中，而是已经意识到：所有的人也正怀着希望，也是我们置身其中的那场运动的一部分。

===Original===
Many people impelled by these ideas have become impatient with the slow recognition on the part of the educators of their manifest obligation to prepare and nourish the child and the citizen for social relations. The educators should certainly conserve the learning and training necessary for the successful individual and family life, but should add to that a preparation for the enlarged social efforts which our increasing democracy requires. The democratic ideal demands of the school that it shall give the child’s own experience a social value; that it shall teach him to direct his own activities and adjust them to those of other people. We are not willing that thousands of industrial workers shall put all of their activity and toil into services from which the community as a whole reaps the benefit, while their mental conceptions and code of morals are narrow and untouched by any uplift which the consciousness of social value might give them.

===Chinese===
被这些观念驱动的许多人，已经对教育者们迟迟认识不到自身显明白白的义务感到不耐：这义务，便是为孩子与公民进入社会关系作预备、施滋养。教育者当然应当保有为成功的个人生活与家庭生活所必需的学识与训练，但在此之外，还应加上一层预备，以应我们日益增长的民主所要求的扩大了的社会努力。民主的理想要求学校赋予孩子自身的经验以社会价值；要求教他引导自己的活动，使之与他人的活动相调适。成千上万的产业工人把全部的活动与辛劳投入整个共同体从中收获利益的服务之中，而他们的心智观念与道德法典却狭隘窄小，不曾受到社会价值意识本可给予他们的任何提升——这是我们不甘愿的。

===Original===
We are impatient with the schools which lay all stress on reading and writing, suspecting them to rest upon the assumption that the ordinary experience of life is worth little, and that all knowledge and interest must be brought to the children through the medium of books. Such an assumption fails to give the child any clue to the life about him, or any power to usefully or intelligently connect himself with it. This may be illustrated by observations made in a large Italian colony situated in Chicago, the children from which are, for the most part, sent to the public schools.

===Chinese===
我们对那种把全部重心放在读写上的学校感到不耐，怀疑它们立足于这样一个假定：生活的寻常经验没有多少价值，一切知识与兴趣都必须经由书本这个媒介传递给孩子。这样的假定既给不了孩子任何通向他四周生活的线索，也给不了他任何把自己有益地、明智地与那生活联结起来的力量。芝加哥有一个庞大的意大利侨民聚居地，其儿童大都被送入公立学校；在那个聚居地所作的观察，可以把这一点说明得很清楚。

===Original===
The members of the Italian colony are largely from South Italy﻿—Calabrian and Sicilian peasants, or Neapolitans from the workingmen’s quarters of that city. They have come to America with the distinct aim of earning money, and finding more room for the energies of themselves and their children. In almost all cases they mean to go back again, simply because their imaginations cannot picture a continuous life away from the old surroundings. Their experiences in Italy have been those of simple outdoor activity, and their ideas have come directly to them from their struggle with Nature﻿—such a hand-to-hand struggle as takes place when each man gets his living largely through his own cultivation of the soil, or with tools simply fashioned by his own hands. The women, as in all primitive life, have had more diversified activities than the men. They have cooked, spun, and knitted, in addition to their almost equal work in the fields. Very few of the peasant men or women can either read or write. They are devoted to their children, strong in their family feeling, even to remote relationships, and clannish in their community life.

===Chinese===
这个意大利侨民聚居地的成员多来自南意大利——卡拉布里亚与西西里的农民，或来自那不勒斯工人居住区的那不勒斯人。他们来到美洲，怀着明确的目标：挣钱，并为他们自己和孩子们的精力觅得更广阔的天地。他们几乎全都打算再回去，只因他们的想象力无法设想一种远离旧环境的连绵生活。他们在意大利的经验，是简单的户外活动的经验；他们的观念直接得自他们与自然的搏斗——那种短兵相接的搏斗：当每个人主要靠自己耕种土地、靠亲手打造的简单工具谋生时，便会有这样的搏斗。同一切初民生活一样，妇女的活动比男子更为多样。除了几乎与男子相等的田间劳作，她们还做饭、纺线、织补。这些农民无论男女，识文断字的寥寥无几。他们疼爱自己的孩子，家族感情浓厚，连远亲也顾念，在共同体生活中抱成一团。

===Original===
The entire family has been upheaved, and is striving to adjust itself to its new surroundings. The men, for the most part, work on railroad extensions through the summer, under the direction of a padrone, who finds the work for them, regulates the amount of their wages, and supplies them with food. The first effect of immigration upon the women is that of idleness. They no longer work in the fields, nor milk the goats, nor pick up faggots. The mother of the family buys all the clothing, not only already spun and woven but made up into garments, of a cut and fashion beyond her powers. It is, indeed, the most economical thing for her to do. Her housecleaning and cooking are of the simplest; the bread is usually baked outside of the house, and the macaroni bought prepared for boiling. All of those outdoor and domestic activities, which she would naturally have handed on to her daughters, have slipped away from her. The domestic arts are gone, with their absorbing interests for the children, their educational value, and incentive to activity. A household in a tenement receives almost no raw material. For the hundreds of children who have never seen wheat grow, there are dozens who have never seen bread baked. The occasional washings and scrubbings are associated only with discomfort. The child of such a family receives constant stimulus of most exciting sort from his city street life, but he has little or no opportunity to use his energies in domestic manufacture, or, indeed, constructively in any direction. No activity is supplied to take the place of that which, in Italy, he would naturally have found in his own surroundings, and no new union with wholesome life is made for him.

===Chinese===
整个家庭被连根拔起，正努力调适于新的环境。男子们大半在夏天修筑铁路延长线，在一个包工头（padrone）的指挥下干活——包工头替他们找活，定他们工资的数目，还供应他们食物。移民对妇女的第一重影响，是闲了下来。她们不再下田，不再挤羊奶，不再拾柴。家里的母亲买来的衣着，不但是纺好织好的，而且已裁制成衣，其式样做工远非她力所能及。这确实也是她最经济的办法。她的洒扫与烹饪简单至极；面包通常在屋外烤好，通心粉买来时已备好待煮。那些户外的与家务的活动——她本会自然而然传给女儿们的——都已从她手边溜走了。家事的手艺没有了，连同它们对孩子们的强烈兴味、它们的教育价值、它们对活动的激励。廉租公寓里的一户人家，几乎收不到任何原材料。几百个从未见过麦子生长的孩子里，就有几十个从未见过面包烤制。偶尔一回的大洗大刷，只与难受相连。这样一家的孩子，从城市街头生活中受到持续的、最令人亢奋的刺激，却几乎或全然没有机会把精力用于家务的制作，实际上也没有机会用于任何建设性的方向。没有任何活动被提供出来，顶替他在意大利本会自然地从自己周围找到的那种活动；也没有人为他缔结与健全生活的新联结。

===Original===
Italian parents count upon the fact that their children learn the English language and American customs before they do themselves, and the children act not only as interpreters of the language, but as buffers between them and Chicago, resulting in a certain almost pathetic dependence of the family upon the child. When a child of the family, therefore, first goes to school, the event is fraught with much significance to all the others. The family has no social life in any structural form and can supply none to the child. He ought to get it in the school and give it to his family, the school thus becoming the connector with the organized society about them. It is the children aged six, eight, and ten, who go to school, entering, of course, the primary grades. If a boy is twelve or thirteen on his arrival in America, his parents see in him a wage-earning factor, and the girl of the same age is already looking toward her marriage.

===Chinese===
意大利的父母们指望的是：孩子们会比他们自己先学会英语和美国习俗；于是孩子们不但充当语言的翻译，还在他们与芝加哥之间充当缓冲，结果是全家对那孩子生出一种近乎令人心酸的依赖。因此，当家里的一个孩子头一回上学时，这件事对所有其他人都意味深长。这个家庭没有任何具有组织形式的社会生活，也拿不出什么给孩子。他理应在学校得到它，再传给他的家庭，学校由此成为他们与四周有组织的社会之间的联结者。上学的是六岁、八岁、十岁的孩子，进的当然是低年级。倘若一个男孩到美洲时已经十二三岁，父母便把他看作一个挣工资的因子；同龄的女孩则已在瞻望自己的婚事。

===Original===
Let us take one of these boys, who has learned in his six or eight years to speak his native language, and to feel himself strongly identified with the fortunes of his family. Whatever interest has come to the minds of his ancestors has come through the use of their hands in the open air; and open air and activity of body have been the inevitable accompaniments of all their experiences. Yet the first thing that the boy must do when he reaches school is to sit still, at least part of the time, and he must learn to listen to what is said to him, with all the perplexity of listening to a foreign tongue. He does not find this very stimulating, and is slow to respond to the more subtle incentives of the schoolroom. The peasant child is perfectly indifferent to showing off and making a good recitation. He leaves all that to his schoolfellows, who are more sophisticated and equipped with better English. His parents are not deeply interested in keeping him in school, and will not hold him there against his inclination. Their experience does not point to the good American tradition that it is the educated man who finally succeeds. The richest man in the Italian colony can neither read nor write﻿—even Italian.
