At the beginning of the next campaign, the greatest difficulty was experienced in finding a candidate, and each one proposed, demanded time to consider the proposition. During this period he invariably became the recipient of the alderman’s bounty. The first one, who was foreman of a large factory, was reported to have been bought off by the promise that the city institutions would use the product of his firm. The second one, a keeper of a grocery and family saloon, with large popularity, was promised the aldermanic nomination on the regular ticket at the expiration of the term of office held by the alderman’s colleague, and it may be well to state in passing that he was thus nominated and successfully elected. The third proposed candidate received a place for his son in the office of the city attorney.

===Chinese===
赫尔会所曾协助对同一位市议员进行过两场不成功的竞选。第一场结束后两年里，当年在运动中露出头角的几乎每个人，都从再度当选的市议员那里得了个官职。一个印刷工人被任命为市政厅的文书；一个车夫因在警察马房服役而领上可观的薪水；候选人本人是个砌砖匠，在市工程局占了个位置。到下一场竞选开场时，最大的难处是找出一个候选人，而每一个被提出的人都要求给他时间考虑。在这段时间里，他照例成为市议员恩赏的对象。第一个是一家大工厂的工头，据说是被这样一个许诺收买的：市政机构将采用他商行的产品。第二个开着一家杂货铺兼家庭酒馆，人望甚高，得到的许诺是：等市议员的同僚任期届满，他将在正规党票上获得市议员提名——顺带说一句，他后来果真这样得到提名并顺利当选。第三个被提的候选人，则为儿子在市法务官的办公室里谋得了一个职位。

===Original===
Not only are offices in his gift, but all smaller favors as well. Any requests to the council, or special licenses, must be presented by the alderman of the ward in which the person desiring the favor resides. There is thus constant opportunity for the alderman to put his constituents under obligations to him, to make it difficult for a constituent to withstand him, or for one with large interests to enter into political action at all. From the Italian peddler who wants a license to peddle fruit in the street, to the large manufacturing company who desires to tunnel an alley for the sake of conveying pipes from one building to another, everybody is under obligations to his alderman, and is constantly made to feel it. In short, these very regulations for presenting requests to the council have been made, by the aldermen themselves, for the express purpose of increasing the dependence of their constituents, and thereby augmenting aldermanic power and prestige.

===Chinese===
不仅官职由他支配，一切较小的恩惠也是如此。凡向议会提出的请求或特别执照，都必须由求惠者居住选区的市议员代为呈递。市议员于是有了不断的机会，使选民欠下自己的人情，使选民难以违逆他，也使身家利益巨大的人根本难以投身政治行动。从想在街面上贩卖水果、求一张执照的意大利小贩，到为把管道从一栋楼通到另一栋楼而想在巷子底下开隧道的大制造公司，人人都欠着他的市议员的人情，而且时时被提醒记得这份情。简言之，这套向议会呈递请求的规程，正是市议员们自己为了扩大选民的依赖、从而增长市议员的权力与威望而特意定下的。

===Original===
The alderman has also a very singular hold upon the property owners of his ward. The paving, both of the streets and sidewalks throughout his district, is disgraceful; and in the election speeches the reform side holds him responsible for this condition, and promises better paving under another regime. But the paving could not be made better without a special assessment upon the property owners of the vicinity, and paying more taxes is exactly what his constituents do not want to do. In reality, “getting them off,” or at the worst postponing the time of the improvement, is one of the genuine favors which he performs. A movement to have the paving done from a general fund would doubtless be opposed by the property owners in other parts of the city who have already paid for the asphalt bordering their own possessions, but they have no conception of the struggle and possible bankruptcy which repaving may mean to the small property owner, nor how his chief concern may be to elect an alderman who cares more for the feelings and pocketbooks of his constituents than he does for the repute and cleanliness of his city.

===Chinese===
市议员对他选区的业主们也有一手非常独特的掌控。他的地段内街道与人行道的铺装糟糕透顶；竞选演说里，改革一方便把这一状况归咎于他，并许诺换一届当局便有更好的铺装。可是，不向附近业主特别摊派，铺装便无从改善，而多缴税款，恰恰是他的选民们最不愿做的事。实际上，替他们「把摊派免掉」，或至少把改善的时日往后推，正是他行的一桩真正的恩惠。一场主张从统筹基金出钱铺路的运动，无疑会遭到城中别处业主的反对——他们已经为自己产业旁的沥青路面付过钱；但他们全然想象不到，重新铺装对小业主可能意味着怎样的挣扎、怎样的倾家荡产，也想象不到，小业主最关切的，也许正是选出一个更顾念选民的心情与钱袋、而不那么顾念城市的名誉与洁净的市议员。

===Original===
The alderman exhibited great wisdom in procuring from certain of his downtown friends the sum of three thousand dollars with which to uniform and equip a boys’ temperance brigade which had been formed in one of the ward churches a few months before his campaign. Is it strange that the good leader, whose heart was filled with innocent pride as he looked upon these promising young scions of virtue, should decline to enter into a reform campaign? Of what use to suggest that uniforms and bayonets for the purpose of promoting temperance, bought with money contributed by a man who was proprietor of a saloon and a gambling house, might perhaps confuse the ethics of the young soldiers? Why take the pains to urge that it was vain to lecture and march abstract virtues into them, so long as the “champion boodler” of the town was the man whom the boys recognized as a loyal and kindhearted friend, the public-spirited citizen, whom their fathers enthusiastically voted for, and their mothers called “the friend of the poor.” As long as the actual and tangible success is thus embodied, marching whether in kindergartens or brigades, talking whether in clubs or classes, does little to change the code of ethics.

===Chinese===
市议员显示出极大的智慧：他从市中心某些朋友那里筹得三千元，用来给一支少年禁酒队置办服装与配备——这支队伍在他竞选之前几个月，成立于本选区的一座教堂。这位善良的领队望着这些前程可期的美德幼苗，心头充满天真的骄傲，要他拒绝参加一场改革竞选，有什么可怪的呢？向他提出：用来促进禁酒的制服与刺刀，其购置款项出自一个酒馆与赌场老板的捐输，或许会搅乱这些小士兵的伦理——这有什么用处？又何必费神力陈：只要全城那位头号「贪贿大王」（boodler）在孩子们眼里仍是一位忠义热肠的朋友、一位热心公务的公民——他们的父亲热情地投他的票，他们的母亲称他「穷人的朋友」——想靠讲课和操练把抽象的美德灌输给他们，便是徒劳。只要切实可见的成功是这样体现于血肉的，那么无论在幼儿园还是在队伍里操步，无论在俱乐部还是在班上宣讲，都改变不了那套伦理法典。

===Original===
The question of where does the money come from which is spent so successfully, does of course occur to many minds. The more primitive people accept the truthful statement of its sources without any shock to their moral sense. To their simple minds he gets it “from the rich” and, so long as he again gives it out to the poor as a true Robin Hood, with open hand, they have no objections to offer. Their ethics are quite honestly those of the merrymaking foresters. The next less primitive people of the vicinage are quite willing to admit that he leads the “gang” in the city council, and sells out the city franchises; that he makes deals with the franchise-seeking companies; that he guarantees to steer dubious measures through the council, for which he demands liberal pay; that he is, in short, a successful “boodler.” When, however, there is intellect enough to get this point of view, there is also enough to make the contention that this is universally done, that all the aldermen do it more or less successfully, but that the alderman of this particular ward is unique in being so generous; that such a state of affairs is to be deplored, of course; but that that is the way business is run, and we are fortunate when a kindhearted man who is close to the people gets a large share of the spoils; that he serves franchised companies who employ men in the building and construction of their enterprises, and that they are bound in return to give work to his constituents. It is again the justification of stealing from the rich to give to the poor. Even when they are intelligent enough to complete the circle, and to see that the money comes, not from the pockets of the companies’ agents, but from the streetcar fares of people like themselves, it almost seems as if they would rather pay two cents more each time they ride than to give up the consciousness that they have a big, warmhearted friend at court who will stand by them in an emergency. The sense of just dealing comes apparently much later than the desire for protection and indulgence. On the whole, the gifts and favors are taken quite simply as an evidence of genuine loving-kindness. The alderman is really elected because he is a good friend and neighbor. He is corrupt, of course, but he is not elected because he is corrupt, but rather in spite of it. His standard suits his constituents. He exemplifies and exaggerates the popular type of a good man. He has attained what his constituents secretly long for.

===Chinese===
这钱花得如此顺手的钱究竟从哪里来，这个问题当然会出现在许多人心头。较为原始的人们听取其来源的真实陈述，道德感毫发无伤。在他们单纯的头脑里，钱是「从富人那里」来的；只要他再像个真正的罗宾汉那样，敞开手把钱散给穷人，他们便毫无异议。他们的伦理，说实在的，便是那些林中欢宴的好汉们的伦理。附近不那么原始的一层人，则满愿意承认：他在市议会里率领着「帮派」，出卖市政特许权；他与谋求特许权的公司做交易；他担保把可疑的议案引渡过议会，为此索取可观的酬金；简言之，他是个成功的「贪贿者」。然而，一旦智力足以达到这一层看法，也便足以提出这样的辩词：这种事人人在做，市议员们个个都干得或多或少有声有色，唯独本选区这位市议员慷慨得独一无二；这种事态固然可叹，但生意就是这么做的，而一个贴近人民的好心人能分到赃款的大头，已是我们的造化；他效力的是那些雇人营建其事业的特许公司，公司反过来也理当给他的选民活计。这又是劫富济贫的辩护。即使他们聪明得足以把圈子画完，看出这钱并非出自公司代理人的口袋，而是出自同他们一样的人们的电车票钱，情况也几乎是：他们宁愿每乘一次车多付两分钱，也不愿放弃那个意识——他们朝中有一位大大的热心肠朋友，急难之时必来撑腰。公正交易的意识，显然来得比寻求保护与纵容的欲望晚得多。大体上，这些馈赠与恩惠被十分简单地当作一种真诚慈爱的凭据。市议员之所以当选，实在是因为他是好朋友、好邻居。他当然腐败，但他并不是因为腐败才当选，倒是不管腐败也照样当选。他的标准正合他的选民。他例示并放大了大众心目中的好人类型。他已赢得了他的选民们暗暗渴求的东西。

===Original===
At one end of the ward there is a street of good houses, familiarly called “Con Row.” The term is perhaps quite unjustly used, but it is nevertheless universally applied, because many of these houses are occupied by professional office holders. This row is supposed to form a happy hunting-ground of the successful politician, where he can live in prosperity, and still maintain his vote and influence in the ward. It would be difficult to justly estimate the influence which this group of successful, prominent men, including the alderman who lives there, have had upon the ideals of the youth in the vicinity. The path which leads to riches and success, to civic prominence and honor, is the path of political corruption. We might compare this to the path laid out by Benjamin Franklin, who also secured all of these things, but told young men that they could be obtained only by strenuous effort and frugal living, by the cultivation of the mind, and the holding fast to righteousness; or, again, we might compare it to the ideals which were held up to the American youth fifty years ago, lower, to be sure, than the revolutionary ideal, but still fine and aspiring toward honorable dealing and careful living. They were told that the career of the self-made man was open to every American boy, if he worked hard and saved his money, improved his mind, and followed a steady ambition. The writer remembers that when she was ten years old, the village schoolmaster told his little flock, without any mitigating clauses, that Jay Gould had laid the foundation of his colossal fortune by always saving bits of string, and that, as a result, every child in the village assiduously collected parti-colored balls of twine. A bright Chicago boy might well draw the inference that the path of the corrupt politician not only leads to civic honors, but to the glories of benevolence and philanthropy. This lowering of standards, this setting of an ideal, is perhaps the worst of the situation, for, as we said in the first chapter, we determine ideals by our daily actions and decisions not only for ourselves, but largely for each other.

===Chinese===
选区一端有一条像样的房子街，人们顺口叫它「骗子街」（Con Row）。这称呼也许用得全然不公，但它毕竟无人不用，因为这些房子里有许多住着职业的官职把持者。这条街被认为构成了成功政客的一块如意猎场：他可以在这里过富足日子，同时又保住在选区里的选票与势力。这一群功成名就、显赫一时的人物——住在那里的市议员也在其内——对附近青年的理想发生过怎样的影响，是很难公允估量的。通往财富与成功、通往市政显要与荣耀的路，便是政治腐败之路。我们不妨把它同本杰明·富兰克林铺出的路作个比较：他同样赢得了这一切，却告诉年轻人，这些唯有靠发奋努力与节俭度日、靠修养心智、靠牢牢守住正道方能取得；或者，我们也可以把它同五十年前悬在美国青年面前的理想作个比较——那理想固然低于革命年代的理想，却仍然美好，仍然向往着体面的交易与谨慎的生活。那时的青年被告知：白手起家者的前程向每一个美国男孩敞开，只要他肯苦干、肯攒钱、肯增进心智、肯抱定一个不移的抱负。作者记得自己十岁时，村塾教师对他那一小群学童毫无折扣地讲过：杰伊·古尔德那宗庞大财富的地基，是靠一贯攒绳头打下的；结果，村里每个孩子都勤勉地攒起五颜六色的线团。一个聪明的芝加哥男孩满可以推出这样的结论：腐败政客的道路不仅通向市政的荣衔，还通向慈善与博爱的荣光。这种标准的降低、这种理想的树立，也许是局势中最坏的一面，因为，如我们在第一章所说，我们是凭每日的行动与抉择来决定理想的，不仅为自己决定，也在很大程度上彼此决定。

===Original===
We are all involved in this political corruption, and as members of the community stand indicted. This is the penalty of a democracy﻿—that we are bound to move forward or retrograde together. None of us can stand aside; our feet are mired in the same soil, and our lungs breathe the same air.

===Chinese===
我们都卷在这场政治腐败之中，作为共同体的成员一体待罪。这是民主的代价——我们注定要一同前进，或一同倒退。谁也不能置身事外；我们的脚陷在同一片泥泞里，我们的肺呼吸着同一种空气。

===Original===
That the alderman has much to do with setting the standard of life and desirable prosperity may be illustrated by the following incident: During one of the campaigns a clever cartoonist drew a poster representing the successful alderman in portraiture drinking champagne at a table loaded with pretentious dishes and surrounded by other revellers. In contradistinction was his opponent, a bricklayer, who sat upon a half-finished wall, eating a meagre dinner from a workingman’s dinner-pail, and the passerby was asked which type of representative he preferred, the presumption being that at least in a workingman’s district the bricklayer would come out ahead. To the chagrin of the reformers, however, it was gradually discovered that, in the popular mind, a man who laid bricks and wore overalls was not nearly so desirable for an alderman as the man who drank champagne and wore a diamond in his shirt front. The district wished its representative “to stand up with the best of them,” and certainly some of the constituents would have been ashamed to have been represented by a bricklayer. It is part of that general desire to appear well, the optimistic and thoroughly American belief, that even if a man is working with his hands today, he and his children will quite likely be in a better position in the swift coming tomorrow, and there is no need of being too closely associated with common working people. There is an honest absence of class consciousness, and a naive belief that the kind of occupation quite largely determines social position. This is doubtless exaggerated in a neighborhood of foreign people by the fact that as each nationality becomes more adapted to American conditions, the scale of its occupation rises. Fifty years ago in America “a Dutchman” was used as a term of reproach, meaning a man whose language was not understood, and who performed menial tasks, digging sewers and building railroad embankments. Later the Irish did the same work in the community, but as quickly as possible handed it on to the Italians, to whom the name “dago” is said to cling as a result of the digging which the Irishman resigned to him. The Italian himself is at last waking up to this fact. In a political speech recently made by an Italian padrone, he bitterly reproached the alderman for giving the-four-dollars-a-day “jobs” of sitting in an office to Irishmen and the-dollar-and-a-half-a-day “jobs” of sweeping the streets to the Italians. This general struggle to rise in life, to be at least politically represented by one of the best, as to occupation and social status, has also its negative side. We must remember that the imitative impulse plays an important part in life, and that the loss of social estimation, keenly felt by all of us, is perhaps most dreaded by the humblest, among whom freedom of individual conduct, the power to give only just weight to the opinion of neighbors, is but feebly developed. A form of constraint, gentle, but powerful, is afforded by the simple desire to do what others do, in order to share with them the approval of the community. Of course, the larger the number of people among whom an habitual mode of conduct obtains, the greater the constraint it puts upon the individual will. Thus it is that the political corruption of the city presses most heavily where it can be least resisted, and is most likely to be imitated.

===Chinese===
市议员在设定生活与称心富足的标准这件事上举足轻重，下面这件小事可以说明：一次竞选期间，一位聪明的漫画家画了一张招贴，画中那位得势的市议员被画成在一桌铺张的菜肴旁喝着香槟，四围尽是同欢的饮者；与之对照的是他的对手，一个砌砖匠，坐在半堵墙上，就着工人的饭盒吃一顿菲薄的饭；过路人被问起愿意要哪一种代表——料想至少在工人地段，砌砖匠总会占先。然而令改革者懊恼的是，人们渐渐发现：在大众心里，一个砌砖、穿工装裤的人做市议员，远不如一个喝香槟、衬衫前襟上别着钻石的人来得可取。这个地段愿意它的代表「同他们中间最出色的人并肩而立」，某些选民当然会以让一个砌砖匠代表自己为耻。这是那种普遍的想显得体面的欲望的一部分，是那种乐观而彻头彻尾美国式的信念：哪怕一个人今天还在用双手干活，在飞速到来的明天，他和他的孩子们多半会处境更好，因此大可不必同普通劳动人民过分亲近。这里有一种诚然无欺的阶级意识的阙如，有一种天真的信念：干什么营生，很大程度上就定了社会地位。在一个外国侨民街坊里，这一点无疑又被夸大了，因为每一个民族越是适应美国的境况，其营生的等级便越上升。五十年前的美国，「荷兰佬」是个骂人的字眼，指一个说话没人听得懂、干着贱役的人——挖阴沟、筑路基。后来爱尔兰人在共同体里干同样的活，但又尽快把它转给了意大利人；据说正因爱尔兰人让给他们的这份挖土活，「达戈」（dago）这个名字才粘在了意大利人身上。意大利人自己也终于醒了过来：一位意大利包工头新近在一次政治演说里愤愤地责备市议员——一天四块钱、坐在办公室里的「差事」给了爱尔兰人，一天一块半、扫街道的「差事」给了意大利人。这场普遍的向上挣扎——至少在政治上，要由职业与社会地位最出色的人来代表——也有它消极的一面。我们必须记得，模仿的冲动在生活中扮演着重要角色；社会评价的丧失，我们人人都痛感，而在最卑微者那里也许最可畏惧，因为在他们中间，个人行止的自由、对邻人意见只给予恰如其分之分量的力量，还只是微弱地发展着。有一种约束，温和而有力，便来自单纯的想要别人做什么自己也做什么的欲望，为的是同他们共享共同体的赞许。当然，一种习常的行为方式在多少人中间通行，它加于个人意志的约束便有多大。城市的政治腐败正是这样：在哪里最无力抵抗，便在哪里压得最重，也最可能在哪里被仿效。

===Original===
According to the same law, the positive evils of corrupt government are bound to fall heaviest upon the poorest and least capable. When the water of Chicago is foul, the prosperous buy water bottled at distant springs; the poor have no alternative but the typhoid fever which comes from using the city’s supply. When the garbage contracts are not enforced, the well-to-do pay for private service; the poor suffer the discomfort and illness which are inevitable from a foul atmosphere. The prosperous business man has a certain choice as to whether he will treat with the “boss” politician or preserve his independence on a smaller income; but to an Italian day laborer it is a choice between obeying the commands of a political “boss” or practical starvation. Again, a more intelligent man may philosophize a little upon the present state of corruption, and reflect that it is but a phase of our commercialism, from which we are bound to emerge; at any rate, he may give himself the solace of literature and ideals in other directions, but the more ignorant man who lives only in the narrow present has no such resource; slowly the conviction enters his mind that politics is a matter of favors and positions, that self-government means pleasing the “boss” and standing in with the “gang.” This slowly acquired knowledge he hands on to his family. During the month of February his boy may come home from school with rather incoherent tales about Washington and Lincoln, and the father may for the moment be fired to tell of Garibaldi, but such talk is only periodic, and the long year round the fortunes of the entire family, down to the opportunity to earn food and shelter, depend upon the “boss.”

===Chinese===
依照同一条法则，腐败政府的实际恶果，注定最沉重地落在最穷、最无能力的人身上。芝加哥的水污浊时，富裕者买远地泉水灌装的瓶装水；穷人别无选择，只能落得因用城市供水而生出的伤寒。垃圾清运合同不被执行时，小康者出钱雇私人清运；穷人则忍受污浊空气必然带来的不适与疾病。富裕的商人尚有几分选择的余地：是同政治「老板」打交道，还是守着较小的收入保住独立；但对一个意大利散工来说，选择只在二者之间：服从政治「老板」的号令，或者实际的饥饿。再者，较有才智的人还可以对眼下的腐败状态作一点达观之思，想想它不过是我们商业主义的一个阶段，我们终将从中走出；无论如何，他还可以拿文学与别的方向的理想自慰；而那个只活在逼仄当下的人却没有这样的凭借：一种信念慢慢进入他的头脑——政治无非是恩惠与位置的事，自治就是讨好「老板」、同「帮派」通同一气。这份慢慢得来的知识，他传给自己的家庭。二月里，他的男孩也许会从学校带回一些语无伦次的华盛顿与林肯的故事，做父亲的一时兴起，也许会讲起加里波第；但这样的谈论只是间或有之，一年到头，全家的命运，直到挣一口饭吃、觅一个遮蔽的机会，都系在「老板」身上。

===Original===
In a certain measure also, the opportunities for pleasure and recreation depend upon him. To use a former illustration, if a man happens to have a taste for gambling, if the slot machine affords him diversion, he goes to those houses which are protected by political influence. If he and his friends like to drop into a saloon after midnight, or even want to hear a little music while they drink together early in the evening, he is breaking the law when he indulges in either of them, and can only be exempt from arrest or fine because the great political machine is friendly to him and expects his allegiance in return.

===Chinese===
在相当程度上，娱乐与消遣的机会也系于他。沿用先前的例子：倘若一个人碰巧嗜赌，倘若老虎机（slot machine）能给他消遣，他便上那些受政治势力保护的场所去。倘若他和朋友们喜欢午夜后溜进酒馆，或者哪怕只想在傍晚早时候对饮时听一点音乐，那么他纵情于其中任何一样，便都犯了法；他之所以能免于被捕或罚款，只因为那部庞大的政治机器对他友好，并指望他的效忠作为回报。

===Original===
During the campaign, when it was found hard to secure enough local speakers of the moral tone which was desired, orators were imported from other parts of the town, from the so-called “better element.” Suddenly it was rumored on all sides that, while the money and speakers for the reform candidate were coming from the swells, the money which was backing the corrupt alderman also came from a swell source; that the president of a streetcar combination, for whom he performed constant offices in the city council, was ready to back him to the extent of fifty thousand dollars; that this president, too, was a good man, and sat in high places; that he had recently given a large sum of money to an educational institution and was therefore as philanthropic, not to say good and upright, as any man in town; that the corrupt alderman had the sanction of the highest authorities, and that the lecturers who were talking against corruption, and the selling and buying of franchises, were only the cranks, and not the solid business men who had developed and built up Chicago.

===Chinese===
竞选期间，本地请不够合意的道德口风的演说者，便从城里别处、从所谓「优秀分子」当中请人来。忽然四下里谣传纷纷：改革候选人的钱和演说家固然出自阔佬，给腐败市议员撑腰的钱同样出自阔佬的来路；一位电车联营公司的总裁——市议员在市议会里为他效力不辍——已备好五万元，随时撑他到底；这位总裁也是好人，身居高位，新近还捐了一笔巨款给一家教育机构，因此其慈善之心——更不必说善良正直——不输城里任何人；腐败的市议员有最高当局的首肯，而那些大谈反对腐败、反对特许权买卖的讲演员，不过是些怪人，并不是那些开发并建设起芝加哥的稳健实业家。

===Original===
All parts of the community are bound together in ethical development. If the so-called more enlightened members accept corporate gifts from the man who buys up the council, and the so-called less enlightened members accept individual gifts from the man who sells out the council, we surely must take our punishment together. There is the difference, of course, that in the first case we act collectively, and in the second case individually; but is the punishment which follows the first any lighter or less far-reaching in its consequences than the more obvious one which follows the second?

===Chinese===
共同体的所有部分，在伦理发展中绑在一起。倘若所谓较开明的成员接受那个收买议会者的公司捐赠，而所谓较不开明的成员接受那个出卖议会者的个人馈赠，我们便注定要一同领受惩罚。区别当然有：前一情形中我们集体行事，后一情形中我们各自行事；但跟着第一种而来的惩罚，难道比跟着第二种而来的、更显眼的惩罚更轻、后果更不深远吗？

===Original===
Have our morals been so captured by commercialism, to use Mr. Chapman’s generalization, that we do not see a moral dereliction when business or educational interests are served thereby, although we are still shocked when the saloon interest is thus served?

===Chinese===
借用查普曼（Chapman）先生的概括：我们的道德是否已被商业主义俘获，以致当商业或教育的利益由此得到服务时，我们竟看不出其中的道德懈怠，尽管酒馆的利益同样由此得到服务时，我们仍会震惊？

===Original===
The streetcar company which declares that it is impossible to do business without managing the city council, is on exactly the same moral level with the man who cannot retain political power unless he has a saloon, a large acquaintance with the semi-criminal class, and questionable money with which to debauch his constituents. Both sets of men assume that the only appeal possible is along the line of self-interest. They frankly acknowledge money getting as their own motive power, and they believe in the cupidity of all the men whom they encounter. No attempt in either case is made to put forward the claims of the public, or to find a moral basis for action. As the corrupt politician assumes that public morality is impossible, so many business men become convinced that to pay tribute to the corrupt aldermen is on the whole cheaper than to have taxes too high; that it is better to pay exorbitant rates for franchises, than to be made unwilling partners in transportation experiments. Such men come to regard political reformers as a sort of monomaniac, who are not reasonable enough to see the necessity of the present arrangement which has slowly been evolved and developed, and upon which business is safely conducted.

===Chinese===
那家宣称不摆平市议会便无法做生意的电车公司，与那个非得有一家酒馆、与半罪犯阶层广相结识、并有来路可疑的钱去败坏选民，否则便保不住政治权力的人，正处在同一个道德水平上。这两种人都假定，唯一可能的诉求只能沿着自利的路线。他们坦率承认挣钱是自己的动力，也笃信他们遇到的一切人个个贪财。两种情形都无意提出公众的权利主张，也无意为行动寻找一个道德基础。正如腐败政客假定公共道德不可能存在，许多商人也渐渐确信：总的来说，向腐败市议员纳贡，比税款过高要便宜；为特许权付出高昂的代价，比被迫充当运输试验的不情愿的合伙人要好。这样的人于是把政治改革者看作一种偏执狂，嫌他们不够通情达理，看不到眼下这套慢慢演变发育起来、生意赖以安稳经营的安排之必要。

===Original===
A reformer who really knew the people and their great human needs, who believed that it was the business of government to serve them, and who further recognized the educative power of a sense of responsibility, would possess a clue by which he might analyze the situation. He would find out what needs, which the alderman supplies, are legitimate ones which the city itself could undertake, in counter-distinction to those which pander to the lower instincts of the constituency. A mother who eats her Christmas turkey in a reverent spirit of thankfulness to the alderman who gave it to her, might be gradually brought to a genuine sense of appreciation and gratitude to the city which supplies her little children with a Kindergarten, or, to the Board of Health which properly placarded a case of scarlet-fever next door and spared her sleepless nights and wearing anxiety, as well as the money paid with such difficulty to the doctor and the druggist. The man who in his emotional gratitude almost kneels before his political friend who gets his boy out of jail, might be made to see the kindness and good sense of the city authorities who provided the boy with a playground and reading room, where he might spend his hours of idleness and restlessness, and through which his temptations to petty crime might be averted. A man who is grateful to the alderman who sees that his gambling and racing are not interfered with, might learn to feel loyal and responsible to the city which supplied him with a gymnasium and swimming tank where manly and well-conducted sports are possible. The voter who is eager to serve the alderman at all times, because the tenure of his job is dependent upon aldermanic favor, might find great relief and pleasure in working for the city in which his place was secured by a well-administered civil service law.

===Chinese===
一位真正了解人民及其巨大人性需要的改革者，一位相信服务人民正是政府本分的改革者，一位进而认识到责任感的教化力量的改革者，将握有一条线索，可以据以剖析这个局面。他会查明：市议员所供应的种种需要中，哪些是正当的、城市自身可以承担的，以别于那些迎合选民低等本能的需要。一位母亲怀着虔敬的感激吃下市议员送的圣诞火鸡，她也可以渐渐被引向对城市的一种真诚的感念与谢意——城市给她的孩子们办了幼儿园；卫生局给隔壁一例猩红热恰当地贴了检疫告示，使她免去了不眠之夜与煎熬的忧虑，也省下了那样吃力地付给医生和药房的钱。一个人出于激动的感激，几乎要跪在把他儿子从牢里搭救出来的政治朋友面前，他也可以被引得看清市政当局的善意与见识——当局为孩子提供了游戏场与阅览室，让他在闲散与躁动之时有处安身，借此躲过小偷小摸的诱惑。一个人感激市议员保障他的赌钱赛马不受搅扰，他也可以学着对城市生出忠心与责任感——城市为他提供了体育馆与游泳池，让有丈夫气的、规矩的运动成为可能。一个选民因为饭碗系于市议员的恩宠而随时乐于为他效劳，他也可以在为城市出力中找到巨大的宽慰与快乐——在这座城市里，他的位置是靠一部施行得当的文官法保住的。

===Original===
After all, what the corrupt alderman demands from his followers and largely depends upon is a sense of loyalty, a standing-by the man who is good to you, who understands you, and who gets you out of trouble. All the social life of the voter from the time he was a little boy and played “craps” with his “own push,” and not with some other “push,” has been founded on this sense of loyalty and of standing in with his friends. Now that he is a man, he likes the sense of being inside a political organization, of being trusted with political gossip, of belonging to a set of fellows who understand things, and whose interests are being cared for by a strong friend in the city council itself. All this is perfectly legitimate, and all in the line of the development of a strong civic loyalty, if it were merely socialized and enlarged. Such a voter has already proceeded in the forward direction in so far as he has lost the sense of isolation, and has abandoned the conviction that city government does not touch his individual affairs. Even Mill claims that the social feelings of man, his desire to be at unity with his fellow-creatures, are the natural basis for morality, and he defines a man of high moral culture as one who thinks of himself, not as an isolated individual, but as a part in a social organism.

===Chinese===
说到底，腐败市议员向他的追随者要求、并在很大程度上仰仗的，是一种忠义之感：站在那个待你好、懂你、把你从麻烦里搭救出来的人一边。这个选民的全部社会生活——从他还是个小男孩、同「自己那伙人」而不是别的什么「帮伙」掷骰子（craps）的时候起——便建立在这种忠义感以及同朋友们抱成一团的感觉上。如今他成人了，他喜欢那种身在政治组织之内的感觉，喜欢人家把政治内闻信托给他，喜欢自己属于一伙懂行的人、而他们的利益正由市议会里一位强有力的朋友照应着的感觉。这一切全然正当，而且全在通向一种坚强的市民忠心的发育线上——只要把它社会化、扩大开去便是。这样的选民既已失却孤绝之感，既已抛弃城市政府与他的私人事务不相干的成见，便已经朝前的方向走了一段。连密尔（Mill）也主张，人的社会性情感、他愿与同胞合一的欲望，是道德的自然基础；他把道德修养高超的人定义为这样的人：他视自己不是孤立的个人，而是社会有机体的一部分。

===Original===
Upon this foundation it ought not to be difficult to build a structure of civic virtue. It is only necessary to make it clear to the voter that his individual needs are common needs, that is, public needs, and that they can only be legitimately supplied for him when they are supplied for all. If we believe that the individual struggle for life may widen into a struggle for the lives of all, surely the demand of an individual for decency and comfort, for a chance to work and obtain the fullness of life may be widened until it gradually embraces all the members of the community, and rises into a sense of the common weal.

===Chinese===
在这块地基上，筑起一座市民德性的建筑，本不该困难。只需向选民讲明白：他的个人需要即是共同的需要，也就是公共的需要；而且只有当它们为所有人一体供应时，为他个人的供应才是正当的。倘若我们相信个人为生存而作的挣扎，可以拓宽为为所有人的生命而作的挣扎，那么，个人对体面与安适、对工作机会与圆满人生的诉求，当然也可以拓宽，直到渐渐拥抱共同体的全体成员，升华为一种共同福祉的意识。

===Original===
In order, however, to give him a sense of conviction that his individual needs must be merged into the needs of the many, and are only important as they are thus merged, the appeal cannot be made along the line of self-interest. The demand should be universalized; in this process it would also become clarified, and the basis of our political organization become perforce social and ethical.

===Chinese===
然而，要使他确信他的个人需要必须融入多数人的需要、并且唯有如此融入才重要，这诉求便不能沿着自利的路线发出。这要求应当被普遍化；在这一过程中，它也将得到澄清，而我们政治组织的基础，便注定成为社会的与伦理的。

===Original===
Would it be dangerous to conclude that the corrupt politician himself, because he is democratic in method, is on a more ethical line of social development than the reformer, who believes that the people must be made over by “good citizens” and governed by “experts”? The former at least are engaged in that great moral effort of getting the mass to express itself, and of adding this mass energy and wisdom to the community as a whole.

===Chinese===
作出这样的结论会有危险吗：腐败政客本人，正因为其方法是民主的，反倒处在比改革者更合伦理的社会发展路线上——改革者相信，人民必须由「好公民」加以改造，由「专家」加以治理？前者至少正投身于那场伟大的道德努力：让大众表达自己，并把大众的能量与智慧，增添给整个共同体。

===Original===
The wide divergence of experience makes it difficult for the good citizen to understand this point of view, and many things conspire to make it hard for him to act upon it. He is more or less a victim to that curious feeling so often possessed by the good man, that the righteous do not need to be agreeable, that their goodness alone is sufficient, and that they can leave the arts and wiles of securing popular favor to the self-seeking. This results in a certain repellent manner, commonly regarded as the apparel of righteousness, and is further responsible for the fatal mistake of making the surroundings of “good influences” singularly unattractive; a mistake which really deserves a reprimand quite as severe as the equally reprehensible deed of making the surroundings of “evil influences” so beguiling. Both are akin to that state of mind which narrows the entrance into a wider morality to the eye of a needle, and accounts for the fact that new moral movements have ever and again been inaugurated by those who have found themselves in revolt against the conventionalized good.

===Chinese===
经验的巨大分歧使好公民难以理解这一观点，又有诸多因素凑在一起，使他难以照此行事。他或多或少是那种奇怪感觉的受害者——好人常常怀有这种感觉：义者无须讨人喜欢，他们的善良本身便足够了，博取民众好感的手段与权谋，尽可留给营私之徒。这便生出某种拒人的做派，通常被看作正义的外袍；它还要对那个致命的错误负责：把「好影响」的环境弄得格外的乏味——这个错误实在该受的斥责，不轻于那件同样可斥的行径：把「恶影响」的环境弄得那样迷人。两者都近似那种把通向更宽广道德的入口收窄成针眼的心境，也解释了这样一个事实：新的道德运动，一再由那些发现自己起而反叛习俗化之善的人们所开创。

===Original===
The success of the reforming politician who insists upon mere purity of administration and upon the control and suppression of the unruly elements in the community, may be the easy result of a narrowing and selfish process. For the painful condition of endeavoring to minister to genuine social needs, through the political machinery, and at the same time to remodel that machinery so that it shall be adequate to its new task, is to encounter the inevitable discomfort of a transition into a new type of democratic relation. The perplexing experiences of the actual administration, however, have a genuine value of their own. The economist who treats the individual cases as mere data, and the social reformer who labors to make such cases impossible, solely because of the appeal to his reason, may have to share these perplexities before they feel themselves within the grasp of a principle of growth, working outward from within; before they can gain the exhilaration and uplift which comes when the individual sympathy and intelligence is caught into the forward intuitive movement of the mass. This general movement is not without its intellectual aspects, but it has to be transferred from the region of perception to that of emotion before it is really apprehended. The mass of men seldom move together without an emotional incentive. The man who chooses to stand aside, avoids much of the perplexity, but at the same time he loses contact with a great source of vitality.

===Chinese===
那位一味坚持行政的纯粹、坚持控制并压制共同体中不驯分子的改革派政治家，其成功可能只是一场收窄而自私的过程的轻易结果。因为，一面借政治机器伺候真正的社会需要，一面又改造那机器使之胜任新的任务——这痛苦的处境，便是要承受过渡到新型民主关系时无可避免的困顿。然而，实际行政中种种令人困窘的经验，自有其真实的价值。把个案仅当作数据的经济学家，和只因诉诸理性便致力于使这类个案不再可能的社会改革者，也许都得分担这些困窘，然后才觉得自己把握住了一条由内向外生长的原理；然后才能赢得那种振奋与升华——当个人的同情与才智被卷入大众向前的直觉运动之中时，它便降临。这场总的运动并非没有其智识的方面，但它必须从知觉的领域转移到情感的领域，然后才真正被领会。大众很少会在没有情感激励的情况下共同行动。选择站在一边的人避开了许多困窘，但同时，他也失去了同一个伟大活力之源的接触。

===Original===
Perhaps the last and greatest difficulty in the paths of those who are attempting to define and attain a social morality, is that which arises from the fact that they cannot adequately test the value of their efforts, cannot indeed be sure of their motives until their efforts are reduced to action and are presented in some workable form of social conduct or control. For action is indeed the sole medium of expression for ethics. We continually forget that the sphere of morals is the sphere of action, that speculation in regard to morality is but observation and must remain in the sphere of intellectual comment, that a situation does not really become moral until we are confronted with the question of what shall be done in a concrete case, and are obliged to act upon our theory. A stirring appeal has lately been made by a recognized ethical lecturer who has declared that “It is insanity to expect to receive the data of wisdom by looking on. We arrive at moral knowledge only by tentative and observant practice. We learn how to apply the new insight by having attempted to apply the old and having found it to fail.”

===Chinese===
对于力图界定并求取一种社会道德的人们，路途上最后的、也是最大的困难或许在于：在他们的努力化为行动、并以某种可行的社会行止或社会控制的形式呈现出来之前，他们无法充分检验这些努力的价值，甚至无法确知自己的动机。因为行动确是伦理的唯一表达媒介。我们不断忘记：道德的领域就是行动的领域；关于道德的思辨只是观察，只能停留在智识评论的领域；一个局面，要到我们面对「在这个具体事例中该做什么」的问题、不得不照我们的理论行动时，才真正成为道德的。一位有声望的伦理学讲演员新近发出了激动人心的呼吁，他宣称：「指望靠旁观领受智慧的数据，是疯狂。我们唯有通过尝试性的、留心观照的实践，才能达致道德知识。我们学会如何运用新的洞见，靠的是曾经尝试运用旧的洞见，并发现它失败。」

===Original===
This necessity of reducing the experiment to action throws out of the undertaking all timid and irresolute persons, more than that, all those who shrink before the need of striving forward shoulder to shoulder with the cruder men, whose sole virtue may be social effort, and even that not untainted by self-seeking, who are indeed pushing forward social morality, but who are doing it irrationally and emotionally, and often at the expense of the well-settled standards of morality.

===Chinese===
这种必须把实验化为行动的必要，把一切怯懦而犹疑的人都甩出了这项事业；更有甚者，还把这样一些人甩了出去：他们在同较为粗粝的人并肩奋进的必要面前畏缩不前——这些人唯一的美德也许就是社会性的努力，就连这美德也未必不染营私之气；他们确乎在推进社会道德，却是以非理性的、情感的方式，而且往往以牺牲确立已久的道德标准为代价。

===Original===
The power to distinguish between the genuine effort and the adventitious mistakes is perhaps the most difficult test which comes to our fallible intelligence. In the range of individual morals, we have learned to distrust him who would reach spirituality by simply renouncing the world, or by merely speculating upon its evils. The result, as well as the process of virtues attained by repression, has become distasteful to us. When the entire moral energy of an individual goes into the cultivation of personal integrity, we all know how unlovely the result may become; the character is upright, of course, but too coated over with the result of its own endeavor to be attractive. In this effort toward a higher morality in our social relations, we must demand that the individual shall be willing to lose the sense of personal achievement, and shall be content to realize his activity only in connection with the activity of the many.

===Chinese===
分辨真正的努力与偶然附生的过失的能力，也许是我们这易谬的才智所要面对的最艰难的考验。在个人道德的范围内，我们已经学会不信靠那种想单靠弃绝尘世、或只对尘世之恶作玄思便达到灵性的人。以压抑求得的德性，其结果与其过程一样，都已令我们生厌。当一个人的全部道德精力都投入个人正直的修养时，我们都知道结果可以何等不可爱；品格当然是端方的，却被自身努力的产物包裹得太厚，以致毫无动人之处。在这场朝向我们社会关系中更高道德的努力里，我们必须要求个人甘愿失去个人成就之感，甘愿只在同多数人的活动的联结中，实现自己的活动。

===Original===
The cry of “Back to the people” is always heard at the same time, when we have the prophet’s demand for repentance or the religious cry of “Back to Christ,” as though we would seek refuge with our fellows and believe in our common experiences as a preparation for a new moral struggle.

===Chinese===
「回到人民去」的呼声，总是同先知悔罪的召唤、同宗教的「回到基督」的呼声一同响起，仿佛我们要在同胞那里避难，要信赖我们共同的经验，把它当作一场新的道德斗争的准备。

===Original===
As the acceptance of democracy brings a certain life-giving power, so it has its own sanctions and comforts. Perhaps the most obvious one is the curious sense which comes to us from time to time, that we belong to the whole, that a certain basic well being can never be taken away from us whatever the turn of fortune. Tolstoy has portrayed the experience in “Master and Man.” The former saves his servant from freezing, by protecting him with the heat of his body, and his dying hours are filled with an ineffable sense of healing and well-being. Such experiences, of which we have all had glimpses, anticipate in our relation to the living that peace of mind which envelopes us when we meditate upon the great multitude of the dead. It is akin to the assurance that the dead understand, because they have entered into the Great Experience, and therefore must comprehend all lesser ones; that all the misunderstandings we have in life are due to partial experience, and all life’s fretting comes of our limited intelligence; when the last and Great Experience comes, it is, perforce, attended by mercy and forgiveness. Consciously to accept Democracy and its manifold experiences is to anticipate that peace and freedom.

===Chinese===
正如对民主的接受会带来某种赋生之力，它也有着自己的认可与安慰。其中最显豁的，也许是那种不时降临我们的奇妙感觉：我们属于整体；无论命运如何翻覆，某种根本的安康永远不会被夺走。托尔斯泰在《主与仆》里描绘过这种经验：主人以自己身体的暖气护住仆人，救他免于冻死，自己弥留的时辰里，则充满一种莫可名状的疗愈与安康之感。这样的经验——我们人人都有过惊鸿一瞥——使我们在对生者的关系里，预尝了那种当我们默想浩浩亡者大军时笼罩心头的安宁。它近乎这样一种确信：亡者是懂得的，因为他们已进入那「大经验」（the Great Experience），故必能领会一切较小的经验；我们在生活中的一切误会皆源于经验的偏狭，一生的焦躁皆出于我们才智的有限；当最后的、大的经验来临时，它注定伴着慈悲与宽恕。有意识地接受民主及其纷繁的经验，便是预尝那份安宁与自由。
